\displaystyle(1-\tfrac{1}{2}\lambda_{q}^{-\gamma})\tilde{D}_{q}\leq\tilde{D}_{q+1},

(3.19)

and in particular deduce, using (
3.9
), (
3.16
) and once more (
3.18
), for any
q\geq q_{I}

\begin{split}\kappa_{q}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\tilde{\rho}_{q}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}dt&=\tilde{D}_{q}\geq\prod_{q^{\prime}=q_{I}}^{q-1}(1-\tfrac{1}{2}\lambda_{q^{\prime}}^{-\gamma})\tilde{D}_{q_{I}}\overset{\textrm{\eqref{e:pwrtail}}}{\geq}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{D}_{q_{I}}\\
&\geq\frac{1}{5}\left(1-\exp(-2\ell^{-2}_{0}\kappa_{q_{I}}T)\right)\|\rho_{in}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=:2c_{0}\|\rho_{in}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,,\end{split}

(3.20)

where we have introduced the constant of the dissipation rate

c_{0}:=\frac{1}{10}\left(1-\exp(-2\ell^{-2}_{0}\kappa_{q_{I}}T)\right)\,.

(3.21)

Step 6. Dissipation in the viscous regime.
Next we
compare the solution
\tilde{\rho}_{q}
of (
3.17
) with the solution
\tilde{\rho}_{u,q}
of

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\tilde{\rho}_{u,q}+u\cdot\nabla\tilde{\rho}_{u,q}=&\kappa_{q}\Delta\tilde{\rho}_{u,q},\\
{\tilde{\rho}_{u,q}}|_{t=0}=&\tilde{\rho}_{in},\end{split}

(3.22)

i.e. with the same diffusion coefficient
\kappa_{q}
and initial datum
\tilde{\rho}_{in}
, but advected by the full Euler flow
u
. Let us rewrite the equation (
3.22
) as

\partial_{t}\tilde{\rho}_{u,q}+u_{q}\cdot\nabla\tilde{\rho}_{u,q}=\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits(\kappa_{q}\nabla\tilde{\rho}_{u,q}+(z-z_{q})\times\nabla\tilde{\rho}_{u,q}),

(3.23)

where
z_{q},z
are the vector potentials of
u_{q},u
. We want to bound
z-z_{q}
from above. To this end write

\|z_{q+1}-z_{q}\|_{C^{0}}\leq\|z_{q+1}-\bar{z}_{q}\|_{C^{0}}+\|\bar{z}_{q}-z_{q}\|_{C^{0}},

where
\bar{z}_{q}
is the vector potential of
\bar{u}_{q}
obtained in Proposition
2.2
. Using the inverse divergence operator
\mathcal{R}
introduced in
[
DLS13
]
((cf. (
7.20
) in Section
7
), the formula (
2.21
) and standard Schauder estimates, we obtain

\|z_{q+1}-\bar{z}_{q}\|_{C^{0}}\leq\|\mathcal{R}w_{q+1}\|_{C^{\alpha}}\lesssim\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1+\alpha}=\lambda_{q}^{-b(1+\beta)+b\alpha}

for some implicit constant depending only on the choice of
\alpha>0
. Next, observe that

-b(1+\beta)+b\alpha=-\theta-b\frac{b-1}{b+1}(1+\beta)+b\alpha,

so that, by choosing
\alpha>0
sufficiently small we deduce

\|z_{q+1}-z_{q}\|_{C^{0}}\lesssim\kappa_{q}\lambda_{q}^{-2\gamma},

(3.24)

where we also used (
2.62
) for estimating
\bar{z}_{q}-z_{q}
.
In particular for any
q\geq q_{0}

\|z-z_{q}\|_{C^{0}}\leq\sum_{q^{\prime}\geq q}\|z_{q+1}-z_{q}\|_{C^{0}}\lesssim\kappa_{q}\lambda_{q}^{-2\gamma}.

In particular, by choosing
a\gg a_{0}
(the base of the double-exponential defining
(\lambda_{q},\delta_{q})
in (
2.3
)) in step 1 sufficiently large, we may conclude the estimate

\|z-z_{q}\|_{C^{0}}\leq\kappa_{q}\lambda_{q}^{-\tfrac{3}{2}\gamma}\quad\textrm{ for any }q\geq q_{0}.

(3.25)

Consequently, for any
q\geq q_{0}
we may apply Proposition
4.3
to (
3.25
) to obtain

|D_{u,q}-D_{q}|\leq\lambda_{q}^{-\gamma}D_{q}\,,

where
D_{u,q}:=\kappa_{q}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\tilde{\rho}_{u,q}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}dt
and
D_{q}:=\kappa_{q}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\tilde{\rho}_{q}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}dt
. In combination with the bound (
3.20
) we then have, for any
q\geq q_{I}
:

\kappa_{q}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\tilde{\rho}_{u,q}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}dt\geq\frac{3}{2}c_{0}\|\rho_{in}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,.

(3.26)

Step 7. Anomalous dissipation for original initial datum.
Finally, we compare
\tilde{\rho}_{u,q}
to the solution of (
1.1
) with the original initial datum
\rho_{in}
, along the sequence
\kappa_{q}
. In other words, we consider

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\rho_{u,q}+u\cdot\nabla\rho_{u,q}=&\kappa_{q}\Delta\rho_{u,q},\\
{\rho_{u,q}}_{|{t=0}}=&\rho_{in}.\end{split}

(3.27)

The basic energy estimate together with (
3.14
) and (
3.10
) gives

\begin{split}\kappa_{q}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho_{u,q}-\nabla\tilde{\rho}_{u,q}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dt&\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\rho_{in}-\tilde{\rho}_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{2}C^{2}_{\tilde{N}}\ell_{in}^{-2}\lambda^{-2}_{q_{I}}\|\rho_{in}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{200}(1-\exp(-2\ell_{0}^{-2}\kappa_{q_{I}}T))\|\rho_{in}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\frac{1}{20}c_{0}\|\rho_{in}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,.\end{split}

Using the Minkowski inequality we deduce, using (
3.26
), for any
q\geq q_{I}
,

\begin{split}\left(\kappa_{q}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho_{u,q}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dt\right)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}&\geq\left(\kappa_{q}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\tilde{\rho}_{u,q}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dt\right)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}-\left(\kappa_{q}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho_{u,q}-\nabla\tilde{\rho}_{u,q}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dt\right)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\\
&\geq\left(\left(\tfrac{3}{2}\right)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}-\left(\tfrac{1}{20}\right)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\right)c_{0}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\|\rho_{in}\|_{L^{2}}\geq c_{0}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\|\rho_{in}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

(3.28)

We conclude

\lim_{q\to\infty}\kappa_{q}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho_{u,q}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dt\geq c_{0}\|\rho_{in}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

and in particular (
3.3
), with constant
c_{0}
given in (
3.21
).

Step 8. The constant
c_{0}
of the dissipation rate.
It remains to verify the expressions for the lower bound of
c_{0}
in terms of
\ell_{0}
,
\ell_{in}
and
T
given in (
3.4
). First of all recall that
q_{I}\geq q_{0}
is chosen according to (
3.10
). Then, observe that, as a function of
T>0
, the left-hand side of (
3.3
) is monotone increasing, so we may assume the same for the lower bound of
c_{0}
.

Next, let us fix
c_{1}=c_{1}(\tilde{N})\geq 1
so that
f(c_{1})\geq(10C_{\tilde{N}})^{2}
, where
C_{\tilde{N}}
is the constant from (
3.11
) and
f
defined in (
3.12
). Set

T_{*}=\ell_{0}^{2}\ell_{in}^{-b\theta}\min\left\{1,(c_{1}\ell_{in}^{-1}\lambda_{q_{0}}^{-1})^{2-b\theta}\right\}\,.

Small time
T
.
If
T<T_{*}
, we choose
q_{I}\geq q_{0}
to be the smallest integer such that

\lambda_{q_{I}}^{2-b\theta}\geq c_{2}\ell_{0}^{2}\ell_{in}^{-2}T^{-1},

(3.29)

where
c_{2}:=c_{1}^{2-b\theta}
. Then

\kappa_{q_{I}+1}\ell_{0}^{-2}T\leq\kappa_{q_{I}+1}\ell_{0}^{-2}T_{*}=\lambda_{q_{I}}^{-b\theta}\ell_{0}^{-2}T_{*}\leq(\lambda_{q_{I}}\ell_{in})^{-b\theta}\leq c_{1}^{-b\theta}\,.

Since the function
s\mapsto 1-e^{-2s}
is concave, we obtain

\begin{split}1-\exp(-2\kappa_{q_{I}+1}\ell_{0}^{-2}T)&\geq\ell_{0}^{-2}\kappa_{q_{I}+1}Tc_{1}^{b\theta}(1-\exp(-2c_{1}^{-b\theta}))\\
&=\ell_{0}^{-2}\lambda_{q_{I}}^{-b\theta}c_{2}^{-1}f(c_{1})T\\
&\geq(10C_{\tilde{N}})^{2}\ell_{0}^{-2}\lambda_{q_{I}}^{-b\theta}c_{2}^{-1}T\end{split}

Therefore, using our choice of
q_{I}
we deduce

(10C_{\tilde{N}})^{2}\ell_{in}^{-2}\leq(10C_{\tilde{N}})^{2}c_{2}^{-1}\ell_{0}^{-2}T\lambda_{q_{I}}^{2-b\theta}\leq\lambda_{q_{I}}^{2}(1-\exp(-2\kappa_{q_{I}+1}\ell_{0}^{-2}T)),

(3.30)

so that (
3.10
) is satisfied. In turn, we obtain the lower bound

c_{0}\geq 10C_{\tilde{N}}^{2}\ell_{in}^{-2}\lambda_{q_{I}}^{-2}.

(3.31)

It remains to estimate
\lambda_{q_{I}}
. To this end observe that the choice of
T_{*}
implies for
T<T_{*}

\lambda_{q_{0}}^{2-b\theta}<c_{2}\ell_{0}^{2}\ell_{in}^{-2}T^{-1},

so that, on the one hand
q_{I}>q_{0}
, and on the other hand, since
\lambda_{q+1}=\lambda_{q}^{b}
and
q_{I}
is chosen to be the smallest with property (
3.29
),

\lambda_{q_{I}}^{2-b\theta}<c_{2}^{b}\ell_{0}^{2b}\ell_{in}^{-2b}T^{-b}.

Applying this to (
3.31
) we obtain

c_{0}\geq C(\tilde{N})\ell_{in}^{-2}\left(\frac{\ell_{in}}{\ell_{0}}\right)^{\frac{4b}{2-b\theta}}T^{\frac{2b}{2-b\theta}}\,.

(3.32)

Large time
T
.
If
T\geq T_{*}
, we may simply take as lower bound the value of the right hand side in (
3.32
) at
T=T_{*}
. This yields

c_{0}\geq C(\tilde{N})\ell_{in}^{-2}\min\{\lambda_{q_{0}}^{-2b},(c_{1}\ell_{in}^{-1})^{-2b}\}.

(3.33)

Combining (
3.32
) and (
3.33
) then yields (
3.4
) with
\delta_{0}:=\lambda_{q_{0}}^{-2b}
. This completes the proof of Theorem
3.1
.

4.
Energy estimates

This section presents energy estimates, used recurrently in the paper. For short notation, in this section we denote sometimes the
L^{2}
(space-time norm) on
\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T]
by
L^{2}_{xt}
. We use
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
to denote the multiindex in a partial derivative and we use
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|
to denote its total order of differentiation. Notation
a\lesssim_{n}b
means that there exists constant
C_{n}
(depending only on
n
) such that
a\leq C_{n}b
. By
D_{t}
we denote the advective derivative, i.e.
D_{t}=\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla
.

4.1.
Estimates for advection-diffusion with Laplacian

We consider the advection-diffusion equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho+u\cdot\nabla\rho

\displaystyle=\kappa\Delta\rho\qquad\textrm{ on }\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T],

(4.1)

\displaystyle\rho|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\rho_{in},

with smooth initial datum.

We assume the scales relationship

\left(\frac{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\kappa}\right)^{n}\geq\left(\frac{\|\nabla^{n}u\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{2n}{n+1}}.

(4.2)

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
\kappa>0
and
u\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})
be divergence free. Assume (
4.2
). Then the advection-diffusion equation (
4.1
) satisfies for any
{t\leq T}
and for any
n\geq 1

\|\nabla^{n}\rho(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla^{n+1}\rho(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\leq\|\nabla^{n}\rho_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+C_{n}\left(\frac{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\kappa}\right)^{n}\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds.

(4.3)

Proof.

Apply
\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
, where
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n
, to the equation and and test the result with
\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
to get after integration in time that for any
{t\leq T}

\frac{1}{2}\|\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla^{n}\rho_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|[u\cdot\nabla,\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}]\rho\|_{L_{xt}^{2}}\|\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho\|_{L_{xt}^{2}}.

For the last term use the commutator estimate

\|[u\cdot\nabla,\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}]f\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{n}\|\nabla^{n}u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla f\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{n}f\|_{L^{2}}

followed by Hölder’s inequality in time to write

\begin{split}\|[u\cdot\nabla,\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}]\rho\|_{L_{xt}^{2}}\|\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho\|_{L_{xt}^{2}}&\lesssim_{n}\|\nabla^{n}u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\rho\|_{L_{xt}^{2}}\|\nabla^{n}\rho\|_{L_{xt}^{2}}+\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{n}\rho\|^{2}_{L_{xt}^{2}}\\
&\lesssim_{n}\|\nabla^{n}u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\rho\|^{\frac{n+1}{n}}_{L_{xt}^{2}}\|\nabla^{n+1}\rho\|^{\frac{n-1}{n}}_{L_{xt}^{2}}+\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\rho\|^{\frac{2}{n}}_{L_{xt}^{2}}\|\nabla^{n+1}\rho\|^{\frac{2n-2}{n}}_{L_{xt}^{2}},\end{split}

where for the latter
\lesssim
we use interpolation. Thus, summing over all partial derivatives of order
n
we have

\begin{split}\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla^{n}\rho(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\kappa\int_{0}^{t}&\|\nabla^{n+1}\rho(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\leq C_{n}\kappa^{-1}\|\nabla^{n}u\|_{L^{\infty}}(\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\rho\|_{L_{xt}^{2}})^{\frac{n+1}{n}}(\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{n+1}\rho\|_{L_{xt}^{2}})^{\frac{n-1}{n}}\\
+C_{n}&\kappa^{-1}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}(\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\rho\|_{L_{xt}^{2}})^{\frac{2}{n}}(\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{n+1}\rho\|_{L_{xt}^{2}})^{\frac{2n-2}{n}}+\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla^{n}\rho_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

Hence, using Young’s inequality we have

\|\nabla^{n}\rho(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla^{n+1}\rho(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\leq\|\nabla^{n}\rho_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+C_{n}\left(\left(\frac{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\kappa}\right)^{n}+\left(\frac{\|\nabla^{n}u\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{2n}{n+1}}\right)\kappa\|\nabla\rho\|_{L_{xt}^{2}}^{2},

which with the assumption (
4.2
) gives (
4.3
).
∎

We will need also the following immediate corollary for the forced advection-diffusion equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho+u\cdot\nabla\rho

\displaystyle=\kappa\Delta\rho+\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits f\qquad\textrm{ on }\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T],

(4.4)

\displaystyle\rho|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\rho_{in},

with smooth initial datum and forcing.

Corollary 4.2
.

Let
\kappa>0
and
u\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})
be divergence free. Assume (
4.2
). Then the forced advection-diffusion equation (
4.4
) satisfies for any
{t\leq T}
and for any
n\geq 1

\begin{split}&\|\nabla^{n}\rho(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla^{n+1}\rho(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\leq\\
&\|\nabla^{n}\rho_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+C_{n}\left(\frac{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\kappa}\right)^{n}\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds+\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n}\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}f(s)\cdot\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho(s)ds\right|.\end{split}

(4.5)

4.2.
Estimates for advection-diffusion with an elliptic matrix

Now we consider the following, more general advection-diffusion equation on
\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T]
, with smooth initial datum

\partial_{t}\rho+u\cdot\nabla\rho=\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits A\nabla\rho,

where
A
is a smooth elliptic matrix.

4.2.1.
Comparison

First, we prove an estimate that allows to compare two solutions whose ellipticity matrices differ slightly from each other.

Proposition 4.3

(Stability estimates)
.

Let
\varrho_{1}
and
\varrho_{2}
solve the following equations on
\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T]

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\varrho_{1}+u\cdot\nabla\varrho_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits(A_{1}\nabla\varrho_{1}),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\varrho_{2}+u\cdot\nabla\varrho_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits(A_{2}\nabla\varrho_{2}),

with initial data
\varrho_{1}(0)=\varrho_{2}(0)=\rho_{\text{in}}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{3})
and uniformly elliptic symmetric matrices
A_{1},A_{2}:\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}
satisfying for
\varepsilon\leq\frac{1}{10}

\displaystyle\big|(A_{1}-A_{2})\xi\cdot\zeta\big|\leq

\displaystyle\varepsilon(A_{1}\xi\cdot\xi)^{\frac{1}{2}}(A_{1}\zeta\cdot\zeta)^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad\text{for any }(x,t)\in\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T]\text{ and }\xi,\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{3}.

(4.6)

Let
\tilde{\varrho}:=\varrho_{1}-\varrho_{2}
, then we have

\displaystyle\|\varrho_{1}(t)-\varrho_{2}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon^{2}\left|\|\varrho_{in}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\|\varrho_{2}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right|,

(4.7)

\displaystyle\left|\|\varrho_{1}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\|\varrho_{2}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 10\varepsilon\left|\|\varrho_{in}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\|\varrho_{2}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right|

(4.8)

for any
t\leq T
.

Proof.

Observe that from (
4.6
), ellipticity, and
\varepsilon\leq\frac{1}{10}
one has the pointwise inequality

|A_{1}\nabla\varrho_{2}\cdot\nabla\varrho_{2}|\leq\tfrac{10}{9}A_{2}\nabla\varrho_{2}\cdot\nabla\varrho_{2}.

(4.9)

Take the difference of the two equations

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\tilde{\varrho}+u\cdot\nabla\tilde{\varrho}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\big(A_{1}\nabla\tilde{\varrho}\big)+\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\big((A_{1}-A_{2})\nabla\varrho_{2}\big)

(4.10)

test it with
\tilde{\varrho}
and integrate by parts. For the resulting last term
\int(A_{1}-A_{2})\nabla\varrho_{2}\cdot\nabla\tilde{\varrho}
use the assumption (
4.6
) and Young’s inequality to absorb the resulting term containing
A_{1}\nabla\tilde{\varrho}
. This gives for any
t\leq T

\|\tilde{\varrho}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{t}\int A_{1}\nabla\tilde{\varrho}\cdot\nabla\tilde{\varrho}\leq\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int A_{1}\nabla\varrho_{2}\cdot\nabla\varrho_{2}.

(4.11)

The inequality (
4.7
) follows directly from (
4.11
) and the energy identity for
\varrho_{2}
.

From the equations for
\varrho_{1}
and
\varrho_{2}
, we can also derive

\partial_{t}\big(\tilde{\varrho}\varrho_{2}\big)=-\tilde{\varrho}u\cdot\nabla\varrho_{2}+\tilde{\varrho}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits(A_{2}\nabla\varrho_{2})\\
-\varrho_{2}u\cdot\nabla\tilde{\varrho}+\varrho_{2}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\big(A_{1}\nabla\tilde{\varrho}\big)+\varrho_{2}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\big((A_{1}-A_{2})\nabla\varrho_{2}\big).

(4.12)

Integrate (
4.12
) over
\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,t]
and integrate by parts in space. The first and the third terms on the right hand side of (
4.12
) cancel and we obtain

\begin{split}-\int&\tilde{\varrho}(t)\varrho_{2}(t)dx=\int_{0}^{t}\int(A_{1}+A_{2})\nabla\tilde{\varrho}\cdot\nabla\varrho_{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\int(A_{1}-A_{2})\nabla\varrho_{2}\cdot\nabla\varrho_{2}\\
&=2\int_{0}^{t}\int A_{1}\nabla\tilde{\varrho}\cdot\nabla\varrho_{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\int(A_{2}-A_{1})\nabla\tilde{\varrho}\cdot\nabla\varrho_{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\int(A_{1}-A_{2})\nabla\varrho_{2}\cdot\nabla\varrho_{2}.\end{split}

(4.13)

For the first right-hand side term of the second line of (
4.13
) we use
|A_{1}\xi\cdot\zeta|\leq(A_{1}\xi\cdot\xi)^{\frac{1}{2}}(A_{1}\zeta\cdot\zeta)^{\frac{1}{2}}
(Cauchy-Schwarz) and Young’s inequality that utilizes
\varepsilon^{2}
of (
4.11
), to get

2\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int A_{1}\nabla\tilde{\varrho}\cdot\nabla\varrho_{2}\right|\leq 2\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int A_{1}\nabla\varrho_{2}\cdot\nabla\varrho_{2}.

For the second right-hand side term of (
4.13
) we use the assumption (
4.6
)
to write

\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int(A_{2}-A_{1})\nabla\tilde{\varrho}\cdot\nabla\varrho_{2}\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int A_{1}\nabla\tilde{\varrho}\cdot\nabla\tilde{\varrho}+\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int A_{1}\nabla\varrho_{2}\cdot\nabla\varrho_{2}\leq\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int A_{1}\nabla\varrho_{2}\cdot\nabla\varrho_{2},

where we have used (
4.11
).
Together with an analogous estimate for the last term of (
4.13
) we obtain

\left|\int\tilde{\varrho}(t)\varrho_{2}(t)dx\right|\leq 4\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int A_{1}\nabla\varrho_{2}\cdot\nabla\varrho_{2}.

Finally we compute, using the above and (
4.11
)

\displaystyle\left|\|\varrho_{1}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\|\varrho_{2}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right|=\left|\int\tilde{\varrho}(t)\big(\varrho_{1}(t)+\varrho_{2}(t)\big)dx\right|\leq\|\tilde{\varrho}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+2\left|\int\tilde{\varrho}(t)\varrho_{2}(t)dx\right|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int A_{1}\nabla\varrho_{2}\cdot\nabla\varrho_{2}+8\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int A_{1}\nabla\varrho_{2}\cdot\nabla\varrho_{2}.

Now the proof of
(
4.8
) follows from (
4.9
) and the energy identity for equation for
\varrho_{2}
.
∎

4.2.2.
General weighted estimate

Estimates in this section are needed mainly in the space homogenization proposition, therefore we use the notation
\bar{\rho},\bar{A}
.
We consider

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bar{\rho}+u\cdot\nabla\bar{\rho}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\bar{A}\nabla\bar{\rho}\qquad\textrm{ on }\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T],

(4.14)

\displaystyle\bar{\rho}|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\rho_{in}.

Let us define

\bar{D}=\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\bar{\rho}(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,ds.

(4.15)

Assume the following inequalities

2\bar{\kappa}(x,t)\mathrm{Id}\geq\bar{A}(x,t)\geq\frac{\bar{\kappa}(x,t)}{2}\mathrm{Id}

(4.16)

and

\left\|\frac{D_{t}\bar{\kappa}}{\bar{\kappa}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\tau^{-1}.

(4.17)

Assume further that for
m\geq 1

\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{m-2}{2}}\nabla^{m}\bar{A}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(\tau^{-1})^{\frac{m}{2}}\qquad\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{m-2}{2}}\nabla^{m}\bar{\kappa}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(\tau^{-1})^{\frac{m}{2}}

(4.18)

and

\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{m-1}{2}}\nabla^{m}u\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\tau^{-1}(\tau^{-1})^{\frac{m-1}{2}}.

(4.19)

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
u
be divergence free. Assume the inequalities (
4.16
), (
4.17
), (
4.19
), (
4.18
)
hold. Then the general advection-diffusion equation (
4.14
) satisfies for any
{t\leq T}
and for any
n\geq 1

\sup_{t\leq T}\|(\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n}{2}}\nabla^{n}\bar{\rho})(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n+1}{2}}\nabla^{n+1}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim(\tau^{-1})^{n}\bar{D}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\tau^{-1})^{n-i}\|(\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{i}{2}}\nabla^{i}\bar{\rho})_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

(4.20)

Furthermore, for any
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n

\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n}{2}}D_{t}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\lesssim(\tau^{-1})^{n+1}\bar{D}+\sum_{i=1}^{n+1}\left(\tau^{-1}\right)^{{n+1-i}}\|(\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{i}{2}}\nabla^{i}\bar{\rho})_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

(4.21)

The constants in
\lesssim
depend on the constants in assumptions and
n
.

Proof.

Step 1: Preliminary
n
-th order estimate
. Apply
\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
, where
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n
, to the equation (
4.14
) and test the result with
\bar{\kappa}^{n}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}
to get

\begin{split}\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}&\int|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n}{2}}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}|^{2}+\int\bar{\kappa}^{n}\bar{A}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}\cdot\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}=\frac{1}{2}\int\frac{D_{t}(\bar{\kappa}^{n})}{\bar{\kappa}^{n}}\bar{\kappa}^{n}|\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}|^{2}\\
&-\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}+{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}={\boldsymbol{\alpha}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}>0}c_{\boldsymbol{\beta,\gamma}}\int\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}u\cdot\partial^{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\nabla\bar{\rho}(\bar{\kappa}^{n}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho})+\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\bar{A}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\bar{\rho}\cdot\nabla(\bar{\kappa}^{n}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho})+\int\bar{A}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}\cdot\nabla(\bar{\kappa}^{n})\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho},\end{split}

(4.22)

where
c_{\boldsymbol{\beta,\gamma}}
are binomial coefficients.
Estimate four right-hand side terms of (
4.22
) in order of their appearance.
For the first one, use
\frac{D_{t}(\bar{\kappa}^{n})}{\bar{\kappa}^{n}}=n\frac{D_{t}\bar{\kappa}}{\bar{\kappa}}
to have

\int\frac{D_{t}(\bar{\kappa}^{n})}{\bar{\kappa}^{n}}\bar{\kappa}^{n}|\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}|^{2}\leq n\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n}{2}}\nabla^{n}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\left\|\frac{D^{u}_{t}\bar{\kappa}}{\bar{\kappa}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}.

For a single summand of the second one we have

\begin{split}\int\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}u\cdot\partial^{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\nabla\bar{\rho}(\bar{\kappa}^{n}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho})&=\int\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n-|\boldsymbol{\gamma}|-1}{2}}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}u\cdot(\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{|\boldsymbol{\gamma}|+1}{2}}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\nabla\bar{\rho})(\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n}{2}}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho})\\
&\leq\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n}{2}}\nabla^{n}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{|\boldsymbol{\gamma}|+1}{2}}\nabla^{|\boldsymbol{\gamma}|+1}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n-|\boldsymbol{\gamma}|-1}{2}}\nabla^{n-|\boldsymbol{\gamma}|}u\right\|_{L^{\infty}}.\end{split}

(4.23)

For a single summand of the third one we write

\begin{split}\int\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\bar{A}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\bar{\rho}\cdot&\nabla(\bar{\kappa}^{n}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho})=n\int\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\bar{A}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\bar{\rho})\bar{\kappa}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla\bar{\kappa}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}+\int\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\bar{A}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\bar{\rho})\bar{\kappa}^{n}\cdot\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}\leq\\
&n\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n-|\boldsymbol{\gamma}|-2}{2}}\nabla^{n-|\boldsymbol{\gamma}|}\bar{A}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\bar{\kappa}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\bar{\kappa}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{|\boldsymbol{\gamma}|+1}{2}}\nabla^{|\boldsymbol{\gamma}|+1}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n}{2}}\nabla^{n}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&+\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n-|\boldsymbol{\gamma}|-2}{2}}\nabla^{n-|\boldsymbol{\gamma}|}\bar{A}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{|\boldsymbol{\gamma}|+1}{2}}\nabla^{|\boldsymbol{\gamma}|+1}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n+1}{2}}\nabla^{n+1}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

(4.24)

For the fourth, last term of (
4.22
) we use the upper bound (
4.16
) and the estimate

\int\bar{\kappa}^{n-1}\bar{A}\nabla\bar{\kappa}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}\cdot\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}\leq 2\int|\bar{\kappa}|^{n}|\nabla\bar{\kappa}||\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}|\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}|\leq 2\|\bar{\kappa}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\bar{\kappa}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n}{2}}\nabla^{n}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n+1}{2}}\nabla^{n+1}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}.

Together, these estimates for four right-hand side terms of (
4.22
), absorbing their terms
\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n+1}{2}}\nabla^{n+1}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}
by the dissipative part using Young’s inequality, after summing over all multiindices of order
n
yield

\begin{split}\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|(\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n}{2}}\nabla^{n}\bar{\rho})(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+&\frac{1}{2}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n+1}{2}}\nabla^{n+1}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim_{n}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n}{2}}\nabla^{n}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\left\|\frac{D_{t}\bar{\kappa}}{\bar{\kappa}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&+\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n}{2}}\nabla^{n}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{j+1}{2}}\nabla^{j+1}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n-j-1}{2}}\nabla^{n-j}u\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&+\|\bar{\kappa}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\bar{\kappa}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n}{2}}\nabla^{n}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n-j-2}{2}}\nabla^{n-j}\bar{A}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{j+1}{2}}\nabla^{j+1}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&+\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n-j-2}{2}}\nabla^{n-j}\bar{A}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{j+1}{2}}\nabla^{j+1}\bar{\rho}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\bar{\kappa}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\bar{\kappa}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n}{2}}\nabla^{n}\bar{\rho}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

(4.25)

Step 2: Plugging in scales assumptions.
Use the assumptions (
4.17
), (
4.18
), (
4.19
) for right-hand side of (
4.25
) to obtain

\frac{d}{dt}\|(\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n}{2}}\nabla^{n}\bar{\rho})(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n+1}{2}}\nabla^{n+1}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim_{n}\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{j+1}{2}}\nabla^{j+1}\bar{\rho}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}(\tau^{-1})^{n-j},

(4.26)

which after integrating in time and writing
P(i)=\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{i}{2}}\nabla^{i}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}
yields for any
n\geq 1

\sup_{t\leq T}\|(\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n}{2}}\nabla^{n}\bar{\rho})(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+P(n+1)\lesssim_{n}\|(\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n}{2}}\nabla^{n}\bar{\rho})_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}P(j+1)(\tau^{-1})^{n-j}.

(4.27)

Step 3: Inductive proof of (
4.20
).
Take (
4.27
) with
n=1
. Observing that
P(1)=\bar{D}
by the definition (
4.15
), we have

\sup_{t\leq T}\|(\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\bar{\rho})(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{\kappa}\nabla^{2}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\tau^{-1}\bar{D}+\|(\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\bar{\rho})_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

(4.28)

i.e. (
4.20
) with
n=1
, which allows to start induction. For the inductive step, assume that (
4.20
) holds for any
j\leq n
, in particular

P(j+1)\lesssim_{n}(\tau^{-1})^{j}\bar{D}+\sum_{i=1}^{j}(\tau^{-1})^{j-i}\|(\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{i}{2}}\nabla^{i}\bar{\rho})_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

For
n+1
we have via (
4.27
)

\begin{split}P(n+2)&\lesssim_{n}\|(\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n+1}{2}}\nabla^{n+1}\bar{\rho})_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\sum_{j=0}^{n}P(j+1)(\tau^{-1})^{n+1-j}\\
&\lesssim_{n}\|(\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n+1}{2}}\nabla^{n+1}\bar{\rho})_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\sum_{j=0}^{n}\left((\tau^{-1})^{j}\bar{D}+\sum_{i=1}^{j}(\tau^{-1})^{j-i}\|(\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{i}{2}}\nabla^{i}\bar{\rho})_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)(\tau^{-1})^{n+1-j},\end{split}

which gives (
4.20
) for
n+1
.

Step 4: Transport estimate.
Apply
\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
, where
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n
, to the equation (
4.14
)

\begin{split}D_{t}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}=-\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}+{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}={\boldsymbol{\alpha}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}>0}c_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}u\cdot\partial^{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\nabla\bar{\rho}+\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}+{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}={\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}c_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\bar{A}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\nabla\bar{\rho}.\end{split}

Multiply both sides with
\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n}{2}}
, distribute the powers of
\bar{\kappa}
appropriately, and take space-time
L^{2}
norms (denoted by
L^{2}_{xt}
) to obtain the following estimate

\begin{split}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n}{2}}D_{t}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{xt}}&\lesssim_{n}\sum_{i+j=n,i>0}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{i-1}{2}}\nabla^{i}u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{j+1}{2}}\nabla^{j+1}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{xt}}\\
&+\sum_{i+j=n}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{i-1}{2}}\nabla^{i+1}\bar{A}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{j+1}{2}}\nabla^{j+1}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{xt}}+\sum_{i+j=n}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{i-2}{2}}\nabla^{i}\bar{A}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{j+2}{2}}\nabla^{j+2}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{xt}}.\end{split}

Use the assumptions (
4.18
), (
4.19
)

\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n}{2}}D_{t}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{xt}}\lesssim_{n}\tau^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{n+2}(\tau^{-1})^{\frac{n-j}{2}}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{j}{2}}\nabla^{j}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{xt}}.

Squaring both sides above yields

\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{n}{2}}D_{t}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{n}(\tau^{-1})^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n+2}(\tau^{-1})^{n-j}P(j).

The estimate (
4.21
) now follows from using (
4.20
) to control
P(j)
.∎

5.
Spatial homogenization

The main result of this section are Proposition
5.1
and its corollaries, which allow to prove the space homogenization step of the key Proposition
2.4
.

In this section, we use
(e_{1},e_{2},e_{3})
to denote the unit Euclidean coordinate vectors. We use
\xi\in\mathbb{T}^{3}
to denote the variable in the cell for homogenization. For a function
f:\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, i.e. taking the variable
(x,t,\xi)\in\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{3}
as its argument, we use
\langle f\rangle
to denote its average in the cell variable
\xi\in\mathbb{T}^{3}
.
We use
\|f(x,t,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{\xi}}
to denote the supremum norm in
\xi
variable. Note that
\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{\xi}}
is still a function in
(x,t)
. With
u
defined below in (
5.1
), we define the transport derivative
D_{t}
on
f
to be

D_{t}f:=\partial_{t}f+(u\cdot\nabla_{x})f.

Without further specification, the
L^{2}
and
H^{-1}
norms are in
x
variable, and the supremum norm
L^{\infty}
is the supremum norm in
(x,t)
.
The constant in
\lesssim
in this section depends on
N_{h}
defined in (
5.12
).

5.1.
Setup

Consider the following advection-diffusion equation for
\rho:\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\rho+u\cdot\nabla\rho=&\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\tilde{A}\nabla\rho,\\
\rho|_{t=0}=&\rho_{\text{in}},\end{split}

(5.1)

with divergence-free
u
and the elliptic tensor
\tilde{A}:\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}
given by

\tilde{A}(x,t):=\sum_{i}\tilde{\eta}_{i}(x,t)\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-1}(x,t)\Bigg(\kappa\mathrm{Id}+\frac{\eta_{i}(x,t)\sigma^{1/2}(t)}{\lambda}\sum_{\vec{k}\in\Lambda}a_{\vec{k}}\left(\tilde{R}_{i}(x,t)\right)H_{\vec{k}}(\lambda\Phi_{i}(x,t))\Bigg)\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-T}(x,t).

(5.2)

The tensor defined in (
5.2
) is the counterpart of
\tilde{A}_{q+1}(x,t)
of (
2.43
) with the
q+1
index dropped. The precise connection between the parameters is given in (
2.55
).

We also define the elliptic tensor in cell variable
\xi
by

A(x,t,\xi):=\sum_{i}\tilde{\eta}_{i}(x,t)\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-1}(x,t)\Bigg(\kappa\mathrm{Id}+\frac{\eta_{i}(x,t)\sigma^{1/2}(t)}{\lambda}\sum_{\vec{k}\in\Lambda}a_{\vec{k}}\left(\tilde{R}_{i}(x,t)\right)H_{\vec{k}}(\xi)\Bigg)\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-T}(x,t).

(5.3)

Our goal in this section is to show the solution
\rho
to (
5.1
) homogenizes to the solution
\bar{\rho}:\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
of the following equation

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\bar{\rho}+u\cdot\nabla\bar{\rho}=&\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\big(\bar{A}\nabla\bar{\rho}\big),\\
\bar{\rho}|_{t=0}=&\rho_{\text{in}},\end{split}

(5.4)

with the elliptic tensor
\bar{A}:\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}
given by

\displaystyle\bar{A}(x,t)=\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int A(x,t,\xi)\Big(\mathrm{Id}+\sum_{i}\eta_{i}(x,t)\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}(x,t)\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{i}^{T}(x,t,\xi)\Big)\,d\xi,

(5.5)

where
\chi_{i},\chi:\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
are given by

\displaystyle\chi_{i}(x,t,\xi)=

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma^{1/2}(t)}{\kappa\lambda}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-1}(x,t)\sum_{\vec{k}\in\Lambda}a_{\vec{k}}\left(\tilde{R}_{i}(x,t)\right)\varphi_{\vec{k}}(\xi)\vec{k},

(5.6)

\displaystyle\chi(x,t,\xi)=

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\chi_{i}(x,t,\xi)\eta_{i}(x,t).

(5.7)

5.1.1.
Assumptions for homogenization

In order to make the homogenization section possibly independent from other sections, in what follows we extract from Section
2
assumptions needed for our quantitative homogenization results.

We introduce the parameters
\kappa,\lambda,\delta,\tau,\ell\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
and
\bar{\kappa}:\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
for which we require

\displaystyle\delta^{1/2}\ell\kappa^{-1}\lambda^{-1}\leq

\displaystyle 1,

(5.8)

\displaystyle\tau\|\bar{\kappa}\|_{L^{\infty}}\ell^{-2}\leq

\displaystyle 1,

(5.9)

\displaystyle\tau\|\bar{\kappa}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lambda^{\frac{2}{b}}\leq

\displaystyle\lambda^{-2\gamma},

(5.10)

\displaystyle\|\bar{\kappa}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{-1}\tau^{-1}\lambda^{-2}\leq\kappa^{-1}\tau^{-1}\lambda^{-2}\leq

\displaystyle\lambda^{-4\gamma}.

(5.11)

We choose
N_{h}\geq 3
sufficiently large such that

\displaystyle N_{h}\left(\frac{b-1}{b+1}\big(1-(2b+1)\beta\big)-\gamma_{T}\right)\geq 2.

(5.12)

We require for the functions
\eta_{i},\tilde{\eta}_{i}:\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{R},\,\sigma:[0,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{R},\,\Phi_{i}:\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{T}^{3},\,a_{\vec{k}}\circ\tilde{R}_{i}:\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
the following conditions

\displaystyle\sigma

\displaystyle\sim\delta,

(5.13)

\displaystyle\|\nabla\Phi_{i}-\mathrm{Id}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{-2\gamma},

(5.14)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\bar{\kappa}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{A}\leq 2\bar{\kappa},\quad\textrm{ where }\bar{\kappa}=\kappa\left(1+\sum_{i}\frac{\sigma\eta_{i}^{2}}{\kappa^{2}\lambda^{2}}\right),

(5.15)

and for
n=0,1
and
m\leq N_{h}

\displaystyle\|\nabla u\|_{C^{m}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2b}}}\lambda^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{b}}}\ell^{-m}

(5.16)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}^{m}D_{t}^{n}\nabla\Phi_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla_{x}^{m}D_{t}^{n}(a_{\vec{k}}\circ\tilde{R}_{i})\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau^{-n}\ell^{-m},

(5.17)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}^{m}D_{t}^{n}\tilde{\eta}_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla_{x}^{m}D_{t}^{n}\eta_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau^{-n},

(5.18)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}^{m}D_{t}^{n}\chi_{i}(x,t,\cdot)\|_{C^{1}_{\xi}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{1/2}}{\kappa\lambda}\tau^{-n}\ell^{-m},

(5.19)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\sigma\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma\tau^{-1}.

(5.20)

Furthermore, we require that
\det\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}=1
and that
\eta_{i}=\eta_{i}(x,t)
,
i\in\mathbb{N}
are smooth nonnegative cutoff functions with pairwise disjoint supports, whereas
(\tilde{\eta}_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
is a partition of unity such that
\tilde{\eta}_{i}\eta_{i}=\eta_{i}
,
\tilde{\eta}_{i}\eta_{j}=0
for
j\neq i
. Finally,
H_{\vec{k}}=H_{\vec{k}}(\xi)
is the antisymmetric zero-mean matrix with the property that for any
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
we have
H_{\vec{k}}v=-U_{\vec{k}}\times v
, where
U_{\vec{k}}=U_{\vec{k}}(\xi)
is given in (
2.19
) (and the functions defining
U_{\vec{k}}
are provided at the beginning of Section
2.1.4
).

Observe that (
5.12
) gives the following relation

\displaystyle\lambda^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\tau\kappa\lambda^{2}\big)^{N_{h}}.

(5.21)

5.2.
Quantitative homogenization estimates

Proposition 5.1
.

Given the assumptions gathered in Section
5.1.1
, let
\rho
be the solution to (
5.1
)-(
5.2
) and
\bar{\rho}
be the solution to (
5.4
)-(
5.6
). Define
\tilde{\rho}:\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
such that

\rho(x,t)=\bar{\rho}(x,t)+\frac{1}{\lambda}\sum_{i}\eta_{i}(x,t)\chi_{i}\big(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i}(x,t)\big)\cdot\nabla\bar{\rho}(x,t)+\tilde{\rho}(x,t).

(5.22)

Then

\begin{split}\sup_{t\leq T}\|\tilde{\rho}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\tilde{\rho}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}dt\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}\kappa\tau}\mathcal{D}_{N_{h}},\\
\text{ where }\quad\mathcal{D}_{l}:=\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\bar{\rho}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}dt+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\tau^{j}\big\|\big(\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{j}{2}}\nabla^{j}\rho\big)_{\text{in}}\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\quad\text{ for }l\in\mathbb{N}.\end{split}

(5.23)

We also have the following corollaries.

Corollary 5.2
.

Let
\rho,\bar{\rho}
and
\mathcal{D}_{l}
be as in Proposition
5.1
, then

\sup_{t\leq T}\|\rho(t)-\bar{\rho}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}\kappa\tau}\mathcal{D}_{N_{h}}.

(5.24)

Corollary 5.3
.

Let
\rho,\bar{\rho}
and
\mathcal{D}_{l}
be as in Proposition
5.1
, then

\begin{split}\sup_{t\leq T}\Big|\|\rho(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}-\|\bar{\rho}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\Big|\lesssim\Big(\frac{1}{\lambda\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\tau^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}+\lambda^{-2\gamma}\Big)\mathcal{D}_{N_{h}}.\\
\end{split}

(5.25)

Corollary 5.4
.

Let
\rho,\bar{\rho}
be as in Proposition
5.1
. Assume for any
j\in[0,N_{h}]

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{j}\rho_{in}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\lambda^{\frac{2j}{b}}\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt,

(5.26)

then

\displaystyle\sup_{t\leq T}\Big|\|\rho(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}-\|\bar{\rho}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\Big|\lesssim\lambda^{-2\gamma}\min\left\{\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\bar{\rho}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}dt,\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt\right\}.

(5.27)

Proof of Corollary
5.4
.

With (
5.26
), we have

\begin{split}\mathcal{D}_{N_{h}}\leq&\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\bar{\rho}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}dt+\sum_{j=1}^{N_{h}}\tau^{j}\|\bar{\kappa}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{j}\lambda^{\frac{2j}{b}}\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt,\\
\overset{\textrm{\eqref{e:hom_para_assump:for_rem}}}{\leq}&\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\bar{\rho}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}dt+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt.\end{split}

(5.28)

Therefore, Corollary
5.4
follows from (
5.25
), (
5.28
) and (
5.11
).
∎

The proofs of Proposition
5.1
and Corollaries
5.2
,
5.3
are given in the end of this section.

The term
\tilde{\rho}
in Proposition
5.1
is the error term in homogenization. In the following two lemmas, in order to estimate
\tilde{\rho}
, we derive and analyse the equation it satisfies.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let the assumptions of Proposition
5.1
hold and let
\tilde{\rho}
be as in (
5.22
). Then
\tilde{\rho}
satisfies

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\tilde{\rho}&+u\cdot\nabla\tilde{\rho}-\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\big(\tilde{A}\nabla\tilde{\rho}\big)=\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\big(\tilde{B}\nabla\bar{\rho}\big)\\
&+\frac{1}{\lambda}\sum_{i}\left(\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\left(\tilde{A}\nabla^{2}\bar{\rho}\eta_{i}\chi_{i}(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i})\right)+\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\left(\tilde{A}\nabla_{x}(\eta_{i}\chi_{i})^{T}(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i})\nabla\bar{\rho}\right)\right)\\
&-\frac{1}{\lambda}\sum_{i}\Big(\eta_{i}\chi_{i}(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i})\cdot D_{t}\nabla\bar{\rho}+\nabla\bar{\rho}\cdot D_{t}(\eta_{i}\chi_{i})(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i})\Big),\end{split}

(5.29)

with the matrices
B:\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}
and
\tilde{B}:\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}
given by

\displaystyle B(x,t,\xi)=

\displaystyle A(x,t,\xi)\Big(\mathrm{Id}+\sum_{i}\eta_{i}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{i}^{T}(x,t,\xi)\Big)-\Big\langle A\Big(\mathrm{Id}+\sum_{i}\eta_{i}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{i}^{T}\Big)\Big\rangle,

(5.30)

\displaystyle\tilde{B}(x,t)=

\displaystyle\tilde{A}\Big(\mathrm{Id}+\sum_{i}\eta_{i}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{i}^{T}(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i})\Big)-\Big\langle A\Big(\mathrm{Id}+\sum_{i}\eta_{i}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{i}^{T}\Big)\Big\rangle.

(5.31)

Proof of Lemma
5.5
.

From the ansatz (
5.22
), omitting the argument
\big(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i}(x,t)\big)
, direct computations give

\displaystyle\nabla\tilde{\rho}

\displaystyle=\nabla\rho-\Big(\mathrm{Id}+\sum_{i}\eta_{i}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{i}^{T}\Big)\nabla\bar{\rho}-\frac{1}{\lambda}\sum_{i}\Big(\nabla^{2}\bar{\rho}\chi_{i}\eta_{i}+\nabla_{x}(\chi_{i}\eta_{i})^{T}\nabla\bar{\rho}\Big),

(5.32)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\tilde{\rho}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\rho-\partial_{t}\bar{\rho}-\sum_{i}\eta_{i}\partial_{t}\Phi_{i}^{T}\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{i}^{T}\nabla\bar{\rho}-\frac{1}{\lambda}\sum_{i}\Big(\partial_{t}\nabla\bar{\rho}\cdot\chi_{i}\eta_{i}+\nabla\bar{\rho}\cdot\partial_{t}(\chi_{i}\eta_{i})\Big).

(5.33)

Using
\partial_{t}\Phi_{i}+(u\cdot\nabla)\Phi_{i}=0
and omitting the argument
\big(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i}(x,t)\big)
, we obtain

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\tilde{\rho}+u\cdot\nabla\tilde{\rho}-\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\tilde{A}\nabla\tilde{\rho}=&\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\left(\left[\tilde{A}\Big(\mathrm{Id}+\sum_{i}\eta_{i}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{i}^{T}\Big)-\Big\langle A\Big(\mathrm{Id}+\sum_{i}\eta_{i}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{i}^{T}\Big)\Big\rangle\right]\nabla\bar{\rho}\right)\\
&+\frac{1}{\lambda}\sum_{i}\Big(\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\left(\tilde{A}\nabla^{2}\bar{\rho}\chi_{i}\eta_{i}\right)+\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\left(\tilde{A}\nabla_{x}(\chi_{i}\eta_{i})^{T}\nabla\bar{\rho}\right)\Big)\\
&-\frac{1}{\lambda}\sum_{i}\Big(\partial_{t}\nabla\bar{\rho}\cdot\chi_{i}\eta_{i}+\partial_{t}(\chi_{i}\eta_{i})\cdot\nabla\bar{\rho}\Big)-\frac{1}{\lambda}\sum_{i}\Big(u\cdot\big(\nabla^{2}\bar{\rho}\chi_{i}\eta_{i}\big)+u\cdot\big(\nabla_{x}\chi_{i}^{T}\eta_{i}\nabla\bar{\rho}\big)\Big)\\
=&\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\big(\tilde{B}\nabla\bar{\rho}\big)+\frac{1}{\lambda}\sum_{i}\Big(\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\left(\tilde{A}\nabla^{2}\bar{\rho}\chi_{i}\eta_{i}\right)+\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\left(\tilde{A}\nabla_{x}(\chi_{i}\eta_{i})^{T}\nabla\bar{\rho}\right)\Big)\\
&-\frac{1}{\lambda}\sum_{i}\Big(\eta_{i}\chi_{i}\cdot D_{t}\nabla\bar{\rho}+\nabla\bar{\rho}\cdot D_{t}(\chi_{i}\eta_{i})\Big).\end{split}

∎

Next, we reformulate the right-hand side of (
5.29
) and provide appropriate estimates. In particular, we need to use potentials of high order (cf.
l_{0}
in what follows), which allows to close estimates in Theorem
1.1
.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let the assumptions of Proposition
5.1
hold and let
\tilde{\rho}
be as in (
5.22
). Then for any
\,l_{0}\leq N_{h}
,
\tilde{\rho}
satisfies

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\tilde{\rho}&+u\cdot\nabla\tilde{\rho}-\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\big(\tilde{A}\nabla\tilde{\rho}\big)=\frac{1}{\lambda}\Big(E_{1}+E_{2}+E_{3}+E_{4}+\sum_{l=1}^{l_{0}}F_{l}+G_{l_{0}}\Big),\end{split}

(5.34)

with

\displaystyle E_{1}=

\displaystyle-\sum_{i,j}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\left(\nabla\partial_{j}\bar{\rho}\times\left(\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}c^{(i)}_{j}(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i})\right)\right),

\displaystyle E_{2}=

\displaystyle-\sum_{i,j,m}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\left(\left(\nabla_{x}c^{(i)}_{jm}(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i})\times\nabla\Phi_{i,m}\right)\partial_{j}\bar{\rho}\right),

\displaystyle E_{3}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\left(\tilde{A}\nabla^{2}\bar{\rho}\eta_{i}\chi_{i}(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i})\right),

\displaystyle E_{4}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\left(\tilde{A}\nabla_{x}\big(\eta_{i}\chi_{i}(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i})\big)^{T}\nabla\bar{\rho}\right)

and

\displaystyle F_{1}=

\displaystyle 0,

(5.35)

\displaystyle F_{l}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{l-1}}\sum_{i,1\leq|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq l-1}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\Big(f^{(l-1)}_{0,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i})D_{t}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}+f^{(l-1)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i})\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}\Big),\text{ for }l\geq 2,

(5.36)

\displaystyle G_{l}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{l-1}}\sum_{i,1\leq|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq l}\Big(g^{(l)}_{0,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i})D_{t}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}+g^{(l)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i})\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}\Big),

(5.37)

for certain functions
c_{j}^{(i)}:\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
f^{(l)}_{0,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}
,
f^{(l)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}
,
g^{(l)}_{0,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}
,
g^{(l)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}:\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, satisfy the following estimates

\displaystyle\langle f^{(l)}_{0,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}\rangle=\langle f^{(l)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle g^{(l)}_{0,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}\rangle=\langle g^{(l)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}\rangle=0,

(5.38)

\displaystyle\|c^{(i)}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\xi}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa\Big(1+\frac{\sigma^{1/2}\eta_{i}}{\kappa\lambda}\Big)\frac{\sigma^{1/2}\eta_{i}}{\kappa\lambda},

(5.39)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}c^{(i)}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\xi}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa\Big(1+\frac{\sigma^{1/2}\eta_{i}}{\kappa\lambda}\Big)\Big(\frac{\sigma^{1/2}\eta_{i}}{\kappa\lambda}\ell^{-1}+\frac{\sigma^{1/2}}{\kappa\lambda}\Big),

(5.40)

\displaystyle\big\|\nabla_{x}^{m}f^{(l)}_{0,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{\xi}}+\big\|\nabla_{x}^{m}g^{(l)}_{0,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{\xi}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{1/2}}{\kappa\lambda}\ell^{-(l-|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|+m)}(\eta_{i}+l),\quad\text{for }l-|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|+m\leq N_{h},

(5.41)

\displaystyle\big\|\nabla_{x}^{m}f^{(l)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{\xi}}+\big\|\nabla_{x}^{m}g^{(l)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{\xi}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{1/2}}{\kappa\lambda}\tau^{-1}\ell^{-(l-|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|+m)}(\eta_{i}+l),\quad\text{for }l-|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|+m\leq N_{h},

(5.42)

and

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm supp}\nolimits f^{(l)}_{0,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i},\mathop{\rm supp}\nolimits f^{(l)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}\mathop{\rm supp}\nolimits g^{(l)}_{0,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i},\mathop{\rm supp}\nolimits g^{(l)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}\subset\mathop{\rm supp}\nolimits\eta_{i},

(5.43)

where
c_{jm}^{(i)}:=c_{j}^{(i)}\cdot e_{m}
and
\nabla\Phi_{i,m}:=\nabla\Phi_{i}\cdot e_{m}
.

Proof of Lemma
5.6
.

Define
\chi_{ij}=\chi_{i}\cdot e_{j}
. Let
b_{j}=Be_{j}
and
\tilde{b}_{j}=\tilde{B}e_{j}
, with
B
and
\tilde{B}
as in Lemma
5.5
. Direct computations show that one can define
b^{(i)}_{j}
such that

\begin{split}\sum_{i}&\eta_{i}b^{(i)}_{j}:=b_{j}(x,t,\xi)\\
=&\sum_{i}\eta_{i}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-1}\Bigg[\kappa\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{ij}+\frac{\sigma^{1/2}}{\lambda}\sum_{\vec{k}\in\Lambda}a_{\vec{k}}(\tilde{R}_{i})\Big(H_{\vec{k}}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-T}e_{j}+\eta_{i}H_{\vec{k}}\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{ij}-\eta_{i}\langle H_{\vec{k}}\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{ij}\rangle\Big)\Bigg].\end{split}

(5.44)

We claim
\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\xi}(\nabla\Phi_{i}b^{(i)}_{j})=0
. Indeed, notice that

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\xi}\big(H_{\vec{k}}\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{ij}\big)=&-\big(W_{\vec{k}}\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\big)\chi_{ij}=-\frac{\sigma^{1/2}}{\kappa\lambda}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-1}\sum_{\vec{k}}a_{\vec{k}}\big(\tilde{R}_{i}\big)\big(\psi_{\vec{k}}\vec{k}\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\big)\varphi_{\vec{k}}\vec{k}\cdot e_{j}=0,\\
\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\xi}\big(H_{\vec{k}}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-T}e_{j}\big)=&-W_{\vec{k}}\cdot\big(\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-T}e_{j}\big)=-\psi_{\vec{k}}\big(\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-1}\vec{k}\big)\cdot e_{j}.\end{split}

(5.45)

Then plugging (
5.45
) and (
5.6
) into (
5.44
) gives
\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\xi}(\nabla\Phi_{i}b^{(i)}_{j})=0
. Since also
\langle\nabla\Phi_{i}b^{(i)}_{j}\rangle=0
, we can find a vector potential
c^{(i)}_{j}=c^{(i)}_{j}(x,t,\xi)
so that
\eta_{i}\nabla\Phi_{i}\,b^{(i)}_{j}=\nabla_{\xi}\times c^{(i)}_{j}
. Using the fact
\det\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}=1
and omitting the argument
\big(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i}(x,t)\big)
, we have

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\lambda}\nabla\times\big(\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}c^{(i)}_{j}\big)&=\frac{1}{\lambda}\nabla\times\big(c^{(i)}_{jm}\nabla\Phi_{i,m}\big)=\frac{1}{\lambda}\nabla c^{(i)}_{jm}\times\nabla\Phi_{i,m}\\
&=\frac{1}{\lambda}\nabla_{x}c^{(i)}_{jm}\times\nabla\Phi_{i,m}+\big(\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}\nabla_{\xi}c^{(i)}_{jm}\big)\times\big(\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}e_{m}\big)\\
&=\frac{1}{\lambda}\nabla_{x}c^{(i)}_{jm}\times\nabla\Phi_{i,m}+\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-1}\big(\nabla_{\xi}\times c^{(i)}_{j}\big),\end{split}

where for the last term we have used the formula
(Av_{1})\times(Av_{2})=A^{-T}(v_{1}\times v_{2})\det A
.
Therefore, omitting the argument
\big(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i}(x,t)\big)
, we have

\displaystyle b_{j}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-1}\nabla_{\xi}\times c^{(i)}_{j}=\sum_{i}\Big(\frac{1}{\lambda}\nabla\times(\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}c^{(i)}_{j})-\frac{1}{\lambda}\nabla_{x}c^{(i)}_{jm}\times\nabla\Phi_{i,m}\Big),

\displaystyle b_{j}\partial_{j}\bar{\rho}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda}\nabla\times\big(\partial_{j}\bar{\rho}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}c^{(i)}_{j}\big)-\frac{1}{\lambda}\nabla\partial_{j}\bar{\rho}\times\big(\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}c^{(i)}_{j}\big)-\frac{1}{\lambda}\big(\nabla_{x}c^{(i)}_{jm}\times\nabla\Phi_{i,m}\big)\partial_{j}\bar{\rho}.

This gives
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
from the first line of (
5.29
). The quantities

and
E_{4}
are given directly by the second line of (
5.29
). The estimate (

). For the estimate (

), one gets a factor

when
\nabla_{x}
hits

. Together with the fact

5.40
).

Next, by induction, we show the last line of (

) equals
\sum_{l=1}^{l_{0}}F_{l}+G_{l_{0}}
for any

, which gives (

). We also show the estimates (

,

, let

, then
F_{1}+G_{1}
equals the the last line of (

), (
5.42
) for

.

Assume (

5.36
), (

) is true for

. To close the induction argument, it suffices to show

\displaystyle G_{l_{0}}=F_{l_{0}+1}+G_{l_{0}+1}

(5.46)

for suitable

and

).
Using chain rule, for any smooth function
h(x,t,\xi):\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
we have

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\lambda}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\bigl(\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-1}h(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i})\bigr)&=(\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\xi}h)(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i})+\frac{1}{\lambda}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{x}(\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-1}h)(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i}).\end{split}

Let
4
4

4

Here it is enough to use the simple inverse divergence
\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\xi}^{-1}=\nabla_{\xi}\Delta_{\xi}^{-1}
.

h_{0}:=\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits^{-1}_{\xi}g^{(l_{0})}_{0,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}
and
h_{1}:=\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits^{-1}_{\xi}g^{(l_{0})}_{1,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}
, then
\langle h_{0}\rangle=\langle h_{1}\rangle=0
. We can deduce (dropping the arguments

)

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\lambda}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\big(\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-1}h_{0}D_{t}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}+&\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-1}h_{1}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}\big)=g^{(l_{0})}_{0,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}D_{t}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}+g^{(l_{0})}_{1,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}\\
&+\frac{1}{\lambda}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{x}\big(\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-1}h_{0}\big)D_{t}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}+\frac{1}{\lambda}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{x}\big(\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-1}h_{1}\big)\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}\\
&+\frac{1}{\lambda}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-1}h_{0}\cdot D_{t}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\nabla\bar{\rho}+\frac{1}{\lambda}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-1}h_{1}\cdot\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\nabla\bar{\rho}.\end{split}

(5.47)

Now let

and

, corresponding to the left hand side of the first line in (

The estimates (

.

Proof of Proposition
5.1
and Corollary
5.2

.

In this proof, for
c_{j}^{(i)}
,
\chi_{i}
,
f^{(l)}_{0,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}
and
g^{(l)}_{0,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}
, we omit the argument
(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i})
as usual.
Test (
5.34
) with
\tilde{\rho}
. By Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality, we have

\begin{split}\sup_{t\leq T}\|\tilde{\rho}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\tilde{\rho}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}dt\lesssim&\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}\kappa}\bigg(\sum_{l=1}^{4}\int_{0}^{T}\|E_{l}\|^{2}_{H^{-1}}dt+\sum_{l=1}^{N}\int_{0}^{T}\|F_{l}\|^{2}_{H^{-1}}dt+\int_{0}^{T}\|G_{N}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}dt\bigg)\\
&+\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\|(\chi\cdot\nabla\bar{\rho})(0)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

(5.48)

In order to estimate the right-hand side terms involving
E_{l}
, we invoke estimates (
4.20
) and (
4.21
) for
\bar{\rho}
from Lemma
4.4
. To verify the assumptions of Lemma
4.4
, note that (
4.18
) and (
4.19
) follow from (
5.9
), (
5.15
), (
5.16
) and direct computations, whereas (
4.16
) and (
4.17
) follow from (
5.15
), (
5.20
) and (
5.17
). Therefore, with
\mathcal{D}_{l}
defined in (
5.23
), we have

\begin{split}\sup_{t\leq T}\|(\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{m-1}{2}}\nabla^{m-1}\bar{\rho})(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{m}{2}}\nabla^{m}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt\lesssim&\tau^{-(m-1)}\mathcal{D}_{m-1},\\
\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{m}{2}}D_{t}\nabla^{m}\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt\lesssim&\tau^{-(m+1)}\mathcal{D}_{m+1}.\end{split}

(5.49)

Observe that (
5.39
) and (
5.40
), together with (
5.8
) and (
5.13
), give for
m\in\{0,1\}

\displaystyle\|\bar{\kappa}^{-1}\nabla_{x}^{m}c^{(i)}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\ell^{-m},

(5.50)

using (
5.19
) and (
5.2
) we obtain for
n\in\{0,1\}
,
m\in\{0,1\}
,

\displaystyle\|\bar{\kappa}^{-1}\tilde{A}D_{t}^{n}\nabla_{x}^{m}\chi\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau^{-n}\ell^{-m}.

(5.51)

Thanks to (
5.50
) and (
5.51
), the estimates for
E_{1}
and
E_{3}
are the same. By the same token, the estimates for
E_{2}
and
E_{4}
are also the same. Here, we estimate
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
as examples. Omitting the argument
(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i})
, we have, using (
5.49
) and
|\nabla\Phi_{i}|<2

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{T}\|E_{1}\|^{2}_{H^{-1}}dt\lesssim&\|\bar{\kappa}^{-1}c^{(i)}_{j}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{\kappa}\nabla^{2}\bar{\rho}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}dt\lesssim\tau^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{1},\\
\int_{0}^{T}\|E_{2}\|^{2}_{H^{-1}}dt\lesssim&\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\|\bar{\kappa}^{-1}\nabla_{x}c^{(i)}_{j}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\bar{\rho}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}dt\\
\lesssim&\|\bar{\kappa}\|_{L^{\infty}}\ell^{-2}\mathcal{D}_{0}\lesssim_{\eqref{e:hom_para_assump:taukappaell}}\tau^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{0}.\end{split}

(5.52)

Next, we estimate
F_{l}
and
G_{N_{h}}
. In the rest of this proof, notice the fact
|\nabla\Phi_{i}|<2
, contributing to a factor finally depending on
N_{h}
. Note that from (
5.41
), (
5.42
), (
5.15
), (
5.8
) and (
5.13
), we have

\begin{split}\big\|\bar{\kappa}^{-\frac{1}{2}}f^{(l)}_{0,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}+\big\|\bar{\kappa}^{-\frac{1}{2}}g^{(l)}_{0,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}&\lesssim\kappa^{-\frac{1}{2}}\ell^{-(l-|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|)},\\
\big\|\bar{\kappa}^{-\frac{1}{2}}f^{(l)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}+\big\|\bar{\kappa}^{-\frac{1}{2}}g^{(l)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}&\lesssim\kappa^{-\frac{1}{2}}\tau^{-1}\ell^{-(l-|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|)}.\end{split}

(5.53)

Note that
F_{1}=0
. For
F_{l}
with
l\geq 2
, using (
5.49
), (
5.53
) and the support condition (
5.43
), we have

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{T}\|F_{l}\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}dt\lesssim&\frac{1}{\lambda^{2(l-1)}}\sum_{m=1,|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=m}^{l-1}\sum_{i}\bigg(\|\bar{\kappa}^{-(m-1)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\bar{\kappa}^{-\frac{1}{2}}f^{(l-1)}_{0,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{m}{2}}D_{t}\nabla^{m}\bar{\rho}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}dt\\
&+\|\bar{\kappa}^{-(m-1)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\bar{\kappa}^{-\frac{1}{2}}f^{(l-1)}_{1,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{m}{2}}\nabla^{m}\bar{\rho}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}dt\bigg)\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda^{2(l-1)}}\sum_{m=1}^{l-1}\|\bar{\kappa}^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{m-1}\kappa^{-1}\ell^{-2(l-1-m)}\tau^{-(m+1)}\mathcal{D}_{m+1}\\
&\lesssim(\lambda\ell)^{-2(l-1)}\kappa\kappa^{-1}\sum_{m=1}^{l-1}\big(\tau\kappa\ell^{-2}\big)^{-m}\tau^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{m+1}\\
&\overset{\eqref{e:hom_para_assump:taukappaell},m=l-1}{\lesssim}(\lambda\ell)^{-2(l-1)}\big(\tau\kappa\ell^{-2}\big)^{-(l-1)}\tau^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{l}\\
&\lesssim\big(\tau\kappa\lambda^{2}\big)^{-(l-1)}\tau^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{l}\\
&\overset{\eqref{e:hom_para_assump:Nh_result},l\geq 2}{\lesssim}\tau^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{l}.\end{split}

(5.54)

For
G_{N_{h}}
, using (
5.49
) and (
5.53
) and the support condition (
5.43
), we have

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{T}\|G_{N_{h}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt&\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda^{2(N_{h}-1)}}\sum_{m=1,|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=m}^{N_{h}}\sum_{i}\bigg(\|\bar{\kappa}^{-(m-1)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\bar{\kappa}^{-\frac{1}{2}}g^{(N_{h})}_{0,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{m}{2}}D_{t}\nabla^{m}\bar{\rho}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}dt\\
&+\|\bar{\kappa}^{-(m-1)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\bar{\kappa}^{-\frac{1}{2}}g^{(N_{h})}_{1,\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{m}{2}}\nabla^{m}\bar{\rho}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}dt\bigg)\\
&\lesssim\lambda^{-2(N_{h}-1)}\sum_{m=1}^{N_{h}}\|\bar{\kappa}^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{m-1}\kappa^{-1}\ell^{-2(N_{h}-m)}\tau^{-(m+1)}\mathcal{D}_{m+1}\\
&\lesssim(\lambda\ell)^{-2(N_{h}-1)}\kappa\kappa^{-1}\ell^{-2}\sum_{m=1}^{N_{h}}\big(\tau\kappa\ell^{-2}\big)^{-m}\tau^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{m+1}\\
&\overset{\eqref{e:hom_para_assump:taukappaell},m=N_{h}}{\lesssim}(\lambda\ell)^{-2(N_{h}-1)}\ell^{-2}\big(\tau\kappa\ell^{-2}\big)^{-N_{h}}\tau^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{N_{h}+1}\\
&\lesssim\lambda^{2}\big(\tau\kappa\lambda^{2}\big)^{-N_{h}}\tau^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{N_{h}+1}\\
&\overset{\eqref{e:hom_para_assump:Nh_result}}{\lesssim}\tau^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{N_{h}+1}.\end{split}

(5.55)

For the last term in (
5.48
) involving initial data, we have

\displaystyle\|(\chi\nabla\bar{\rho})(0)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\bar{\kappa}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\chi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\rho_{\text{in}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\kappa^{-1}\|\bar{\kappa}^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\rho_{\text{in}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\kappa^{-1}\tau^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{1}.

(5.56)

This finishes the proof of Proposition
5.1
.

For the proof of Corollary
5.2
, observe that in addition to the already obtained estimates, we have to control only the corrector term, i.e. the term of (
5.22
) involving
\chi
. Its estimate is analogous to (
5.56
) via (
5.49
). This concludes our proofs.
∎

Proof of Corollary
5.3
.

To simplify the formulas, we introduce the following shorthand notation

\displaystyle M(x,t,\xi):=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Id}+\sum_{i}\eta_{i}(x,t)\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}(x,t)\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{i}^{T}\big(x,t,\xi\big)

(5.57)

and denote by
\tilde{M}(x,t)
the matrix
M(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i})
(analogously to
A
and
\tilde{A}
, cf. (
5.3
) and (
5.2
)).
We first claim the following estimates

\displaystyle\left|\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int\big(\tilde{M}^{T}\tilde{M}-\langle\tilde{M}^{T}\tilde{M}\rangle\big)\nabla\bar{\rho}\cdot\nabla\bar{\rho}\right|\lesssim

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{1}{\lambda\ell}+\frac{1}{\lambda\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\tau^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\Big)\mathcal{D}_{1},

(5.58)

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa}{\lambda^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla^{2}\bar{\rho}\chi\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla_{x}\chi^{T}\nabla\bar{\rho}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}\kappa\tau}\mathcal{D}_{1}.

(5.59)

Before proving these claims, let us conclude the proof of the Corollary
5.3
.
First, direct computations give

\displaystyle\tilde{M}^{T}\tilde{M}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Id}+\sum_{i}\eta_{i}(\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{i}^{T}+\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{i}\nabla\Phi_{i})+\sum_{i}\eta_{i}^{2}\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{i}\nabla\Phi_{i}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{i}^{T},

\displaystyle\tilde{M}^{T}\tilde{M}-\langle\tilde{M}^{T}\tilde{M}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\eta_{i}(\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{i}^{T}+\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{i}\nabla\Phi_{i})+

\displaystyle+\sum_{i}\eta_{i}^{2}(\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{i}\nabla\Phi_{i}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{i}^{T}-\langle\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{i}\nabla\Phi_{i}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{i}^{T}\rangle).

Furthermore, by using (
5.6
) and the properties of Mikado flows in Section
2.1.4
, we compute

\displaystyle\kappa\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{i}\nabla\Phi_{i}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{i}^{T}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\sum_{\vec{k}\in\Lambda}a_{\vec{k}}^{2}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-1}(\vec{k}\otimes\nabla_{\xi}\varphi_{\vec{k}})\nabla\Phi_{i}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}(\nabla_{\xi}\varphi_{\vec{k}}\otimes\vec{k})\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-T}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\sum_{\vec{k}\in\Lambda}a_{\vec{k}}^{2}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-1}|\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}\nabla_{\xi}\varphi_{\vec{k}}|^{2}(\vec{k}\otimes\vec{k})\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-T}.

In particular, comparing with the derivation of
\bar{A}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\big|\kappa\langle\tilde{M}^{T}\tilde{M}\rangle-\bar{A}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i}\tilde{\eta}_{i}\|\nabla\Phi_{i}-\mathrm{Id}\|_{L^{\infty}}|\bar{A}|\lesssim\lambda^{-2\gamma}|\bar{A}|.

(5.60)

Here we use (
5.6
), (
5.14
), (
5.15
) and the formula for the homogenized matrix
\bar{A}
(compare the first line of the formula (
2.49
)).

Next, using (
5.32
) and omitting
(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i})
, we have for an absolute constant
C>1

\displaystyle\kappa

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt=\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\Big\|\tilde{M}\nabla\bar{\rho}+\frac{1}{\lambda}(\nabla^{2}\bar{\rho}\chi+\nabla_{x}\chi^{T}\nabla\bar{\rho})+\nabla\tilde{\rho}\Big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(1+\frac{1}{\lambda\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\tau^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\Big)\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\tilde{M}\nabla\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt+\big(1+C\lambda\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\tau^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\big)\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\tilde{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt

\displaystyle+\big(1+C\lambda\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\tau^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\big)\frac{\kappa}{\lambda^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla^{2}\bar{\rho}\chi\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla_{x}\chi^{T}\nabla\bar{\rho}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}dt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(1+\frac{1}{\lambda\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\tau^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\Big)\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int\big(\tilde{M}^{T}\tilde{M}-\langle\tilde{M}^{T}\tilde{M}\rangle\big)\nabla\bar{\rho}\cdot\nabla\bar{\rho}dxdt+\Big(1+\frac{1}{\lambda\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\tau^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\Big)\int_{0}^{T}\int\bar{A}\nabla\bar{\rho}\cdot\nabla\bar{\rho}dxdt

\displaystyle+\Big(1+\frac{1}{\lambda\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\tau^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\Big)\int_{0}^{T}\int\big(\kappa\langle\tilde{M}^{T}\tilde{M}\rangle-\bar{A}\big)\nabla\bar{\rho}\cdot\nabla\bar{\rho}dxdt+\big(1+C\lambda\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\tau^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\big)\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int\|\nabla\tilde{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt

\displaystyle+\big(1+C\lambda\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\tau^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\big)\frac{\kappa}{\lambda^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla^{2}\bar{\rho}\chi\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla_{x}\chi^{T}\nabla\bar{\rho}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{C}{\lambda\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\tau^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}+\frac{1}{\lambda\ell}+\lambda^{-2\gamma}\Big)\mathcal{D}_{N_{h}}+\Big(1+\frac{C}{\lambda\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\tau^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\Big)\int_{0}^{T}\int\bar{A}\nabla\bar{\rho}\cdot\nabla\bar{\rho}dxdt,

where for the last line we used, respectively, (
5.58
), (
5.60
), Proposition
5.1
and (
5.59
). Now we can conclude the proof of Corollary
5.3
with

\displaystyle\left|\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt-\int_{0}^{T}\int\bar{A}\nabla\bar{\rho}\cdot\nabla\bar{\rho}dxdt\right|\lesssim\Big(\frac{1}{\lambda\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\tau^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}+\frac{1}{\lambda\ell}+\lambda^{-2\gamma}\Big)\mathcal{D}_{N_{h}}.

It remains to prove the claims (
5.58
), (
5.59
). The proof of (
5.59
) is the same as estimates for the terms
E_{3}
and
E_{4}
in the proof of Proposition
5.1
, so we omit the details. For the claim (
5.58
), we find a matrix potential
\omega:=\sum_{i}\eta_{i}\omega^{(i)}
taking arguments
(x,t,\xi)
such that

\displaystyle\big(M^{T}M-\langle M^{T}M\rangle\big)_{jl}=

\displaystyle\Delta_{\xi}\omega_{jl}=\sum_{i}\eta_{i}\Delta_{\xi}\omega_{jl}^{(i)},

(5.61)

\displaystyle\big\|\nabla_{\xi}\omega_{j}\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{\xi}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\Big(\frac{\sigma\eta_{i}^{2}}{\kappa^{2}\lambda^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{1/2}\eta_{i}}{\kappa\lambda}\Big)\lesssim\sum_{i}\Big(1+\frac{\sigma\eta_{i}^{2}}{\kappa^{2}\lambda^{2}}\Big)\lesssim\frac{\bar{\kappa}}{\kappa},

(5.62)

\displaystyle\big\|\nabla_{x}\nabla_{\xi}\omega_{j}\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{\xi}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\Big(\frac{\sigma\eta_{i}}{\kappa^{2}\lambda^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{1/2}}{\kappa\lambda}\Big)(\eta_{i}\ell^{-1}+1)\lesssim\sum_{i}\Big(1+\frac{\sigma\eta_{i}^{2}}{\kappa^{2}\lambda^{2}}\Big)\ell^{-1}\lesssim\frac{\bar{\kappa}}{\kappa\ell}.

(5.63)

The estimates (
5.62
) and (
5.63
) can be proved in the same way as the estimates (
5.39
) and (
5.40
) in Lemma
5.6
. Now by the chain rule, for any
i,j,l
, we have

(\Delta_{\xi}\omega_{jl}^{(i)})(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i})=\frac{1}{\lambda}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\bigl(\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-1}\nabla_{\xi}\omega_{jl}^{(i)}(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i})\bigr)-\frac{1}{\lambda}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{x}(\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-1}\nabla_{\xi}\omega_{jl}^{(i)})(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i}).

(5.64)

Consider the left-hand side of the wanted (
5.58
) and use the formulas (
5.61
), (
5.64
), omitting the argument
(x,t,\lambda\Phi_{i})
, to write

\begin{split}\kappa\int_{0}^{T}&\int\big((\tilde{M}^{T}\tilde{M})-\langle\tilde{M}^{T}\tilde{M}\rangle\big)\nabla\bar{\rho}\cdot\nabla\bar{\rho}dxdt\\
=&\frac{\kappa}{\lambda}\sum_{i,j,l}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int\eta_{i}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\bigl(\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-1}\nabla_{\xi}\omega_{jl}^{(i)}\bigr)\partial_{j}\bar{\rho}\partial_{l}\bar{\rho}dxdt-\int_{0}^{T}\int\eta_{i}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{x}\bigl(\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-1}\nabla_{\xi}\omega_{jl}^{(i)}\bigr)\partial_{j}\bar{\rho}\partial_{l}\bar{\rho}dxdt\Big)\\
=&\frac{\kappa}{\lambda}\sum_{i,j,l}\bigg(-\int_{0}^{T}\int\bigl(\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-1}\nabla_{\xi}\omega_{jl}^{(i)}\bigr)\cdot\nabla_{x}\big(\eta_{i}\partial_{j}\bar{\rho}\partial_{l}\bar{\rho}\big)dxdt-\int_{0}^{T}\int\eta_{i}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{x}\bigl(\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-1}\nabla_{\xi}\omega_{jl}^{(i)}\bigr)\partial_{j}\bar{\rho}\partial_{l}\bar{\rho}dxdt\bigg).\end{split}

(5.65)

Now, we estimate the terms on the right-hand side of (
5.65
) using (
5.62
), (
5.17
), and Young’s inequality with the splitting
\bar{\kappa}|\nabla\bar{\rho}||\nabla^{2}\bar{\rho}|=(\tau^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}}\bar{\kappa}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}}|\nabla\bar{\rho}|)(\tau^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}}\bar{\kappa}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}}|\nabla^{2}\bar{\rho}|)
. Consequently we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int\big((\tilde{M}^{T}\tilde{M})-\langle\tilde{M}^{T}\tilde{M}\rangle\big)\nabla\bar{\rho}\cdot\nabla\bar{\rho}dxdt\right|

\displaystyle\overset{{\eqref{c:hom_dissipation_eq7},\eqref{e:hom_assump:slow}}}{\lesssim}\frac{1}{\lambda\ell}\int_{0}^{T}\int\bar{\kappa}|\nabla\bar{\rho}|^{2}dxdt+\frac{1}{\lambda}\int_{0}^{T}\int\bar{\kappa}|\nabla\bar{\rho}||\nabla^{2}\bar{\rho}|dxdt

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda\ell}\int_{0}^{T}\int\bar{\kappa}|\nabla\bar{\rho}|^{2}dxdt+\frac{1}{\lambda\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\tau^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\int_{0}^{T}\int\bar{\kappa}|\nabla\bar{\rho}|^{2}dxdt+\frac{\tau^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\int_{0}^{T}\int|\bar{\kappa}\nabla^{2}\bar{\rho}|^{2}dxdt

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{p:hom_trho:p:e3}}{\lesssim}\Big(\frac{1}{\lambda\ell}+\frac{1}{\lambda\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\tau^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\Big)\mathcal{D}_{1},

which is the estimate (
5.58
).
∎

6.
Time averaging

The main result of this section is Proposition
6.1
, which allows to prove the time averaging step of the key Proposition
2.4
.

6.1.
Setup

Consider constants
\kappa_{1}>\kappa_{0}>0
, and the oscillatory
\tilde{\kappa}=\tilde{\kappa}(x,t,\tau^{-1}t)
of the form

\tilde{\kappa}(x,t,\tau^{-1}t)=\kappa_{0}+\kappa_{1}\,\eta(x,t,\tau^{-1}t),

(6.1)

where
s\mapsto\eta(x,t,s)
is a
1
-periodic, nonnegative and smooth function with

\langle\eta\rangle:=\int_{0}^{1}\eta(x,t,s)\,ds=1\,.

We write

\kappa:=\kappa_{0}+\kappa_{1},\quad g(x,t,s):=\frac{\kappa_{1}}{\kappa}(\eta(x,t,s)-1),\quad g_{\tau}(x,t):=g(x,t,\tau^{-1}t),

(6.2)

so that
\langle g\rangle=0
,
g
is bounded, and we have the identity

\tilde{\kappa}(x,t,\tau^{-1}t)=\kappa_{0}+\kappa_{1}\eta(x,t,\tau^{-1}t)=\kappa+\kappa g_{\tau}(x,t).

(6.3)

In this section we consider the solutions
\rho
and
\bar{\rho}
of the following equations

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\rho+u\cdot\nabla\rho&=\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\left(\tilde{\kappa}\nabla\rho\right),\\
\rho|_{t=0}&=\rho_{in},\end{split}

(6.4)

and

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\bar{\rho}+u\cdot\nabla\bar{\rho}&=\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\left(\kappa\nabla\bar{\rho}\right),\\
\bar{\rho}|_{t=0}&=\rho_{in}.\end{split}

(6.5)

In view of definitions of
\kappa
,
\tilde{\kappa}
, the equation (
6.5
) can be seen as the time-averaged version of (
6.4
).
Further, we define the respective total dissipations

\tilde{D}:=\int_{0}^{T}\|\tilde{\kappa}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\nabla\rho\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dt\,,\quad D:=\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dt.

(6.6)

We make the following assumptions:

There exists
\mu\geq\|\nabla u\|_{\infty}
(we should think of
\mu^{-1}\sim\|\nabla u\|_{\infty}^{-1}
being the advection time-scale) such that

(A1)

The fast time-scale is shorter than the advection time-scale, i.e.

\tau\mu<1/2.

(6.7)

In relation to (
6.7
), we fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that

(\tau\mu)^{N-1}\kappa<\kappa_{0}.

(6.8)

(A2)

(Control of higher-order spatial derivatives) For any
n\leq N

\|\nabla u\|_{C^{n}}^{2}\leq C_{N}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{n}\mu^{2}.

(6.9)

(A3)

(Bound on the initial data) For any
1\leq n\leq N

\|\rho_{in}\|_{H^{n}}^{2}\leq C_{N}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{n}D.

(6.10)

(A4)

(Control of cutoff and its slow derivatives) For any
1\leq n\leq N

\|\nabla^{n}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C_{N}(\tau\mu)^{2}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{n}{2}},\qquad\|D^{slow}_{t}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C_{N}\mu,

(6.11)

where for a function
\eta(x,t,s)
we define its slow advective derivative as follows

D^{slow}_{t}\eta(x,t,s):=\partial_{t}\eta(x,t,s)+u\cdot\nabla\eta(x,t,s).

(6.12)

6.2.
Time averaging proposition

Proposition 6.1
.

Under the assumptions (A1), (A2), (A3), (A4) we have

\sup_{t\leq T}\|\rho(t)-\bar{\rho}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho-\nabla\bar{\rho}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,dt\lesssim(\tau\mu)^{2}D.

(6.13)

Moreover, the total dissipation satisfies

|D-\tilde{D}|\lesssim(\tau\mu)(D+\tilde{D}).

(6.14)

The implicit constants depend only on
N
,
C_{N}
in assumptions, and on
\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}
.

Proof.

Starting with
\rho^{(0)}:=\bar{\rho}
being the solution of (
6.5
), we construct the solution
\rho
of (
6.4
) by successive approximations
\rho^{(i)}
,
i=1,2,\dots,N
, defined to be solutions of

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\rho^{(i)}+u\cdot\nabla\rho^{(i)}&=\kappa\Delta\rho^{(i)}+\kappa\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\left(g_{\tau}\nabla\rho^{(i-1)}\right),\\
\rho|_{t=0}&=\rho_{in}.\end{split}

(6.15)

We will show in Lemma
6.2
the improved energy bound

\sup_{t\leq T}\|\rho^{(i)}(t)-\rho^{(i-1)}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho^{(i)}-\nabla\rho^{(i-1)}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,dt\leq C_{N}(\tau\mu)^{2i}D,

(6.16)

see (
6.28
) for
n=0
.
Setting

\rho^{(error)}:=\rho-\rho^{(N)}

and using (
6.4
), (
6.15
) and (
6.3
), we may write the equation for
\rho^{(error)}
as

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\rho^{(error)}+u\cdot\nabla\rho^{(error)}&=\kappa\Delta\rho^{(error)}+\kappa\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits(g_{\tau}\nabla\rho^{(error)})+\kappa\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits(g_{\tau}(\nabla\rho^{(N)}-\nabla\rho^{(N-1)}))\,\\
&=\kappa_{0}\Delta\rho^{(error)}+{\kappa_{1}}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits(\eta\nabla\rho^{(error)})+\kappa\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits(g_{\tau}(\nabla\rho^{(N)}-\nabla\rho^{(N-1)})),\end{split}

where for the second line we used
\kappa+\kappa g_{\tau}=\kappa_{0}+\kappa_{1}\eta
.
Since
\eta\geq 0
and
|g|\leq 2
, the standard energy estimate and an application of Young’s inequality yields

\sup_{t\leq T}\|\rho^{(error)}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\kappa_{0}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho^{(error)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dt\leq C\frac{\kappa}{\kappa_{0}}\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho^{(N)}-\nabla\rho^{(N-1)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dt.

Combining this with the improved energy bound (
6.16
) and (A1) we deduce

\begin{split}\sup_{t\leq T}\|\rho^{(error)}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho^{(error)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dt&\leq C_{N}\left(\frac{\kappa}{\kappa_{0}}\right)^{2}(\tau\mu)^{2N}D\leq C_{N}(\tau\mu)^{2}D\,.\end{split}

(6.17)

Consequently, since
\rho^{(0)}:=\bar{\rho}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\leq T}\|\rho(t)-\bar{\rho}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho-\nabla\bar{\rho}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\,dt\leq 2\|\rho(T)-\rho^{(N)}(T)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+2\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho-\nabla\rho^{(N)}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,dt

\displaystyle+2^{N}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}

\displaystyle\|\rho^{(i+1)}(T)-\rho^{(i)}(T)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho^{(i+1)}-\nabla\rho^{(i)}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,dt

\displaystyle\lesssim(\tau\mu)^{2}D\,,

verifying (
6.13
).

Next, we consider the difference in total dissipation

D-\tilde{D}=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\kappa|\nabla\bar{\rho}|^{2}-\tilde{\kappa}|\nabla\rho|^{2}\,dx\,dt=\underbrace{-\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}g_{\tau}|\nabla\bar{\rho}|^{2}\,dx\,dt}_{(I)}+\underbrace{\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\tilde{\kappa}(|\nabla\bar{\rho}|^{2}-|\nabla\rho|^{2})\,dx\,dt}_{(II)}.

We will show in Lemma
6.2
(see (
6.29
)) the bound

|(I)|\lesssim(\tau\mu)D.

Indeed, in the notation introduced for Lemma
6.2
(cf. (
6.20
)), it holds
(I)=B^{(0)}(\nabla\rho^{(0)},\nabla\rho^{(0)})
.
Next, we use Young’s inequality and
\tilde{\kappa}\lesssim\kappa
to estimate

\displaystyle|(II)|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\tilde{\kappa}(\nabla\rho+\nabla\bar{\rho})\cdot(\nabla\rho-\nabla\bar{\rho})\,dx\,dt\right|\,

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho-\nabla\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dt+\varepsilon\left(\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dt+\int_{0}^{T}\|\tilde{\kappa}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\nabla\rho\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dt\right).

Choosing
\varepsilon=\tau\mu
and using (
6.13
) we deduce

|(II)|\lesssim(\tau\mu)(D+\tilde{D}),

verifying (
6.14
).
∎

6.3.
Estimates for successive approximations

This section is concerned with estimates for the successive approximations (
6.15
), resulting in the improved energy bound (
6.16
) and in (
6.29
), used in the proof of Proposition
6.1
. Since the improved energy bound may be of separate interest, we present it independently from the specific choices made in the previous section. To this end let us observe that the definition (
6.2
) of
g
and of
\eta

imply that there exists the fast time potential i.e.
G(x,t,s)=\int_{0}^{s}g(x,t,a)da
such that

\partial_{s}G(x,t,s)=g(x,t,s).

6.3.1.
Notation

Let us denote
g_{\tau}(x,t):=g(x,t,\tau^{-1}t)
. We introduce

g^{0}:=g,\;G^{0}:=G,\;\text{ and inductively }\;g^{l+1}:=G^{l}g^{0},\;\partial_{s}G^{l}=g^{l}.

Hence
G^{l}=\frac{1}{(l+1)!}(G^{0})^{(l+1)}
.
Furthermore, let us write

D:=\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho_{0}(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

(6.18)

\VE f\VE^{2}_{n}:=\sup_{t\leq T}\|(\nabla^{n}f)(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla^{n+1}f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt+\left(\frac{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\kappa}\right)^{n}\left(\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt\right),

(6.19)

B^{l}(f,h):=\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int g^{l}_{\tau}f\cdot h.

(6.20)

6.3.2.
Commutators

We will need several estimates for commutators. Let
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n
, then it holds

\|[g\cdot\nabla,\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}]f\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{n}\|\nabla^{n}g\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla f\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla g\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{n}f\|_{L^{2}},

(6.21)

\|[\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}},g]\nabla f\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{n}\|\nabla^{n}g\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla f\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla g\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{n}f\|_{L^{2}}.

(6.22)

6.3.3.
Improved energy bound

Observe that

g_{\tau}^{l}=\tau D_{t}(G^{l}_{\tau})-\tau(D^{slow}_{t}G^{l})_{\tau}.

(6.23)

Since
\frac{\kappa_{1}}{\kappa}\leq 1
and
\eta
is bounded, Assumption (A4) and definitions yield via a direct computation that for
f=g^{l}
or
f=G^{l}
we have
5
5

5

In fact, the estimates (
6.24
) can be made
l
-independent, which results in estimate (
6.29
) uniform in
l
. Since we use in Proposition
6.1
merely the case
l=0
of (
6.29
), its dependence on
l
plays no role.

\begin{split}&\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim_{l}1,\qquad\|(D^{slow}_{t}f)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim_{l}\mu,\\
&\text{and for }n>0\qquad\|\nabla^{n}f\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim_{n,l}(\tau\mu)^{2}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{n}{2}}.\end{split}

(6.24)

Consider

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\rho^{(i)}+u\cdot\nabla\rho^{(i)}&-\kappa\Delta\rho^{(i)}=\kappa\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\left(g_{\tau}\nabla\rho^{(i-1)}\right),\\
\rho^{(i)}|_{t=0}&=0\;\text{ for }i>0,\qquad\rho^{(0)}|_{t=0}=\rho_{in}\end{split}

(6.25)

and
\rho^{(-1)}\equiv 0
.

Lemma 6.2
.

Assume that there is
\partial_{s}G(x,t,s)=g(x,t,s)
such that (
6.24
) holds for both
f=g
,
f=G
.
Assume (
6.9
), (
6.10
), (
6.11
).
Then the energy solutions of (
6.25
) satisfy:

(1) for
\rho^{(0)}

\|\rho^{(0)}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+2\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\rho^{(0)}(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,ds=\|\rho_{in}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

(6.26)

for any
t\in[0,T]
. Moreover, for any
n\geq 1

\VE\rho^{(0)}\VE^{2}_{n}\lesssim_{n}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{n}D.

(6.27)

(2) for
\rho^{(i)}
,
i>0
,
n\geq 0

\begin{split}\VE\rho^{(i)}\VE^{2}_{n}\lesssim_{n,i}(\tau\mu)^{2i}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{n}D.\end{split}

(6.28)

Furthermore

\begin{split}&\sup_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|=m}\left|B^{l}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i)},\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(i)})\right|\lesssim_{n,m,i,l}(\tau\mu)^{2i+1}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{n+m}{2}}D.\end{split}

(6.29)

Note that the equation (
6.15
) with Assumptions (A2)-(A4) falls within the above lemma.

Proof.

Step 1: Preliminary bound for the bilinear term.
Let
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n
and
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|=m
. It holds by definition and (
6.23
)

\begin{split}B^{l}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i)},&\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)})=\kappa\tau\int_{0}^{T}\int(D_{t}(G^{l}_{\tau})-(D^{slow}_{t}G^{l})_{\tau})\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i)}\cdot\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)}\\
=&\kappa\tau\int G^{l}_{\tau}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i)}\cdot\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)}\bigg|_{t=0}^{t=T}-\kappa\tau\int_{0}^{T}\int G^{l}_{\tau}D_{t}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i)}\cdot\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)})\\
&-\kappa\tau\int_{0}^{T}(D^{slow}_{t}G^{l})_{\tau}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i)}\cdot\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)}.\\
\end{split}

(6.30)

For the time boundary term of (
6.30
