), integrating once by parts in space and using (
6.24
) we write

\left|\int G^{l}_{\tau}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i)}\cdot\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)}\bigg|_{t=0}^{t=T}\right|\lesssim\VE\rho^{(i)}\VE_{n}\VE\rho^{(j)}\VE_{m+2}+(\tau\mu)\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\VE\rho^{(i)}\VE_{n}\VE\rho^{(j)}\VE_{m+1}.

For the last term of (
6.30
) we simply write the bound

\kappa\tau\left|\int_{0}^{T}(D^{slow}_{t}G^{l})_{\tau}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i)}\cdot\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)}\right|\leq\tau\|D^{slow}_{t}G^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}}\VE\rho^{(i)}\VE_{n}\VE\rho^{(j)}\VE_{m}\lesssim\tau\mu\VE\rho^{(i)}\VE_{n}\VE\rho^{(j)}\VE_{m}.

Together, for any
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n
and
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|=m

\begin{split}\left|B^{l}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i)},\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)})\right|\lesssim\kappa\tau\VE\rho^{(i)}\VE_{n}\VE\rho^{(j)}\VE_{m+2}+\kappa\tau(\tau\mu)\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\VE\rho^{(i)}\VE_{n}\VE\rho^{(j)}\VE_{m+1}\\
+\tau\mu\VE\rho^{(i)}\VE_{n}\VE\rho^{(j)}\VE_{m}+\kappa\tau\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int G^{l}_{\tau}D_{t}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i)}\cdot\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)})\right|.\end{split}

(6.31)

Step 2: Reformulating energy estimates.
The case
i=0
(nonzero initial datum, zero forcing since
\rho^{(-1)}\equiv 0
) is the energy estimate of Lemma
4.1
, together with the assumption (
6.10
).

For
i>0
,
the energy estimate with forcing (Corollary
4.2
) gives (zero initial datum for
i>0
)

\begin{split}\sup_{t\leq T}&\|\nabla^{n}\rho^{(i)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\kappa\|\nabla^{n+1}\rho^{(i)}\|_{L_{xt}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim_{n}\\
&\kappa\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n}\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}(g_{\tau}\nabla\rho^{(i-1)})\cdot\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i)}\right|+\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{n}\kappa\|\nabla\rho^{(i)}\|_{L_{xt}^{2}}^{2},\end{split}

(6.32)

where in case
n=0
the second term vanishes. We rewrite it as

\begin{split}\VE\rho^{(i)}\VE^{2}_{n}\lesssim_{n}&\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n}\left|B^{0}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i-1)},\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i)})\right|+\kappa\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n}\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int([g_{\tau}\cdot\nabla,\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}]\rho^{(i-1)})\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i)}\right|\\
+&\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{n}\kappa\|\nabla\rho^{(i)}\|_{L_{xt}^{2}}^{2},\end{split}

where in case
n=0
only the first term is present. Using for the last right-hand side term of the preceding inequality its case with
n=0
we obtain

\begin{split}\VE\rho^{(i)}\VE^{2}_{n}\lesssim&\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n}\left|B^{0}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i-1)},\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i)})\right|+\kappa\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n}\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int([g_{\tau}\cdot\nabla,\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}]\rho^{(i-1)})\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i)}\right|+\\
&\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{n}\left|B^{0}(\nabla\rho^{(i-1)},\nabla\rho^{(i)})\right|.\end{split}

(6.33)

The commutator estimate (
6.21
) and Young’s inequality allow to bound the middle right-hand side term of (
6.33
) as follows

\begin{split}&\kappa\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n}\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int([g_{\tau}\cdot\nabla,\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}]\rho^{(i-1)})\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i)}\right|\\
&\leq\frac{1}{C}\kappa\|\nabla^{n+1}\rho^{(i)}\|^{2}_{L_{xt}^{2}}+C\kappa\|\nabla^{n}g\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\rho^{(i-1)}\|^{2}_{L_{xt}^{2}}+C\kappa\|\nabla g\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{n}\rho^{(i-1)}\|^{2}_{L_{xt}^{2}},\end{split}

so absorbing the
\nabla^{n+1}
term into left-hand side of (
6.33
) and using (
6.24
) we arrive at

\begin{split}\VE\rho^{(i)}\VE^{2}_{n}&\lesssim_{n}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{n}\left|B^{0}(\nabla\rho^{(i-1)},\nabla\rho^{(i)})\right|+\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n}\left|B^{0}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i-1)},\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i)})\right|\\
+&(\tau\mu)^{2}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{n}\kappa\|\nabla\rho^{(i-1)}\|^{2}_{L_{xt}^{2}}+(\tau\mu)^{2}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)\kappa\|\nabla^{n}\rho^{(i-1)}\|^{2}_{L_{xt}^{2}},\end{split}

(6.34)

where in case
n=0
only the first term is present.

Step 3: Setting up induction.
Consider the second line of (
6.34
): (i) it agrees with the desired estimate (
6.28
) for
i=1
, if we use there definition (
6.18
) and (
6.27
); (ii) moreover, if we knew the estimate (
6.28
) for
\rho^{(i-1)}
, it agrees with it for
\rho^{(i)}
.
Therefore, if we knew that for
\boldsymbol{\alpha}=0
and
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n
it holds

\left|B^{0}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i-1)},\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i)})\right|\lesssim(\tau\mu)^{2i}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{k}D,

(6.35)

then (
6.28
) can be shown by induction.
It is clear then that we should prove our (
6.28
) inductively, keeping track of
B
.

We induce over
i=0,1,\dots
. The inductive assumption is that for any
n,m,l

\begin{split}(a)\qquad&\VE\rho^{(j)}\VE^{2}_{n}\lesssim(\tau\mu)^{2j}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{n}D\qquad\forall_{j\leq i},\\
(b)\qquad&\sup_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|=m}\left|B^{l}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(j)},\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(k)})\right|\lesssim(\tau\mu)^{j+k+1}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{n+m}{2}}D\qquad\forall_{j,k\leq i}.\end{split}

(6.36)

(where constants in
\lesssim
may depend on
n,m,l,i
),
and we want to show it for
i+1
.

Step 4: Initializing the induction,
i=0
.
At the beginning of Section
6.3.3
we have already shown (
6.26
), (
6.27
) for
i=0
, so the part (a) of the inductive assuption for
i=0
holds. We need to show now
i=0
for the part (b). From (
6.31
) with
i=0
it holds, thanks to the already known part (a)

\begin{split}\left|B^{l}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(0)},\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(0)})\right|\lesssim\tau\mu\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{n+m}{2}}D+\kappa\tau\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int G^{l}_{\tau}D_{t}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(0)}\cdot\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(0)})\right|\end{split}

(6.37)

for any
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n
,
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|=m
.
We focus on the last term of (
6.37
). Since via (
6.15
) for
\rho^{(0)}
(no forcing) we have

D_{t}(\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(0)})=\kappa\Delta\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(0)}+[u\cdot\nabla,\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}]\rho^{(0)},

it holds

\kappa\tau\int_{0}^{T}\int G^{l}_{\tau}D_{t}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(0)})\cdot\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(0)}=\kappa\tau\int_{0}^{T}\int G^{l}_{\tau}(\kappa\Delta\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(0)}+[u\cdot\nabla,\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}]\rho^{(0)})\cdot\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(0)}.

(6.38)

For the first right-hand side term of (
6.38
) we use (
6.24
) and (
6.27
) to write

\begin{split}\kappa\tau\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int G^{l}_{\tau}(\kappa\Delta\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(0)}\cdot\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(0)}\right|&\lesssim\kappa\tau\left(\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{n+3}\rho^{(0)}\|_{L_{xt}^{2}}\right)\left(\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{m+1}\rho^{(0)}\|_{L_{xt}^{2}}\right)\\
&\lesssim\kappa\tau\VE\rho^{(0)}\VE_{n+2}\VE\rho^{(0)}\VE_{m}\lesssim(\tau\mu)\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{n+m}{2}}D.\end{split}

For the second right-hand side term of (
6.38
), using the assumption (
6.24
), the commutator estimate (
6.21
) (for
n+1
), and then (
6.27
) we write

\begin{split}&\kappa\tau\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int G^{l}_{\tau}[u\cdot\nabla,\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}]\rho^{(0)}\cdot\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(0)}\right|\\
&\lesssim\tau\left(\|\nabla^{n+1}u\|_{L^{\infty}}\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\rho^{(0)}\|_{L_{xt}^{2}}+\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{n+1}\rho^{(0)}\|_{L_{xt}^{2}}\right)\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{m+1}\rho^{(0)}\|_{L_{xt}^{2}}\\
&\lesssim\tau\left(\|\nabla^{n+1}u\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{n}{2}}\right)\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{m}{2}}D\lesssim(\tau\mu)\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{n+m}{2}}D,\end{split}

where the last inequality follows from the scales assumption (
6.9
). Hence the entire right-hand side of (
6.38
) is estimated by
(\tau\mu)\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{n+m}{2}}D
. This and the other estimate with
D_{t}
acting on
\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho_{0}
(performed analogously) gives via (
6.37
) the bound

\sup_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|=m}\left|B^{l}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(0)},\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(0)})\right|\lesssim(\tau\mu)\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{n+m}{2}}D,

(6.39)

which is the inductive hypothesis for
i=0
.

Step 5: The inductive step
i\to i+1
.
Now we assume (
6.36
) and want to show (for any
n
,
m
,
l
)

\begin{split}(a^{\prime})\qquad&\VE\rho^{(i+1)}\VE^{2}_{n}\lesssim(\tau\mu)^{2i+2}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{n}D,\\
(b^{\prime})\qquad&\sup_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|=m}\left|B^{l}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)},\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(i)})\right|\lesssim(\tau\mu)^{2i+2}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{n+m}{2}}D.\end{split}

(6.40)

Substep 5.1: Estimate of
B^{l}
in terms of
\rho^{(i+1)}
.
For any
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n
,
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|=m
the preliminary inequality (
6.31
) for
i+1
,
j\leq i
gives via part (a) of the inductive assumption (
6.36
)

\begin{split}&\left|B^{l}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)},\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)})\right|\lesssim\VE\rho^{(i+1)}\VE_{n}(\tau\mu)^{j+1}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{m}{2}}D^{\frac{1}{2}}+\kappa\tau\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int G^{l}_{\tau}D_{t}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)}\cdot\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)})\right|.\end{split}

(6.41)

We will focus on the last term of (
6.41
). By (
6.15
) for
\rho^{(k)}
(arbitrary natural
k
), we have

D_{t}(\partial^{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\rho^{(k)})=\kappa\Delta\partial^{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\rho^{(k)}+[u\cdot\nabla,\partial^{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}]\rho^{(k)}+\kappa\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\partial^{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(g\nabla\rho^{(k-1)}\right).

(6.42)

Now we consider two cases, depending on what
D_{t}
in the last term of
(
6.41
) acts on.

The case when
D_{t}
in the last term of (
6.41
) acts on
\rho^{(j)}
. Use (
6.42
) with
k=j
to write

\begin{split}&\kappa\tau\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int G^{l}_{\tau}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)}\cdot D_{t}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)}\right|=\\
&\kappa\tau\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int G^{l}_{\tau}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)}\cdot\left(\kappa\Delta\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)}+[u\cdot\nabla,\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}]\rho^{(j)}+\kappa\nabla\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\left(g_{\tau}\nabla\rho^{(j-1)}\right)\right)\right|=\\
&\kappa\tau\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int G^{l}_{\tau}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)}\cdot(\kappa\Delta\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)}+[u\cdot\nabla,\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}]\rho^{(j)}+\kappa g_{\tau}\nabla\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\nabla\rho^{(j-1)}+\kappa[\nabla\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}},g_{\tau}]\nabla\rho^{(j-1)}\right|\\
&\lesssim\kappa\tau\VE\rho^{(i+1)}\VE_{n}\left(\VE\rho^{(j)}\VE_{m+2}+\frac{\|\nabla^{m+1}u\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\kappa}\VE\rho^{(j)}\VE_{0}+\frac{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\kappa}\VE\rho^{(j)}\VE_{m}\right)\qquad=:I\\
&+\kappa\tau\VE\rho^{(i+1)}\VE_{n}\left(\|\nabla^{m+2}g\|_{L^{\infty}}\VE\rho^{(j-1)}\VE_{0}+\|\nabla g\|_{L^{\infty}}\VE\rho^{(j-1)}\VE_{m+1}\right)\qquad=:II\\
&+\kappa^{2}\tau\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int G^{l}_{\tau}g_{\tau}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)}\cdot\nabla\Delta\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j-1)}\right|\qquad=:III.\end{split}

(6.43)

The inequality in (
6.43
) holds via commutator estimates (
6.21
) and (
6.22
).

For the three terms on the right-hand side of (
6.43
) we use the inductive assumption (a) and assumption (
6.9
) to bound them as follows

I\lesssim\VE\rho^{(i+1)}\VE_{n}(\tau\mu)^{j+1}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{m}{2}}D^{\frac{1}{2}},

for the second one additionally using (
6.24
)

II\lesssim\VE\rho^{(i+1)}\VE_{n}(\tau\mu)^{j+1}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{m}{2}}D^{\frac{1}{2}},

and we keep the term
III
of (
6.43
), written as
\kappa\tau\left|B^{l+1}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)},\nabla\Delta\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j-1)})\right|
, in accordance with the definition of the bilinear form
B^{l}
.
These two estimates used in (
6.43
) show that

\begin{split}&\kappa\tau\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int G^{l}_{\tau}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)}\cdot D_{t}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)}\right|\\
&\lesssim\VE\rho^{(i+1)}\VE_{n}(\tau\mu)^{j+1}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{m}{2}}D^{\frac{1}{2}}+\kappa\tau\left|B^{l+1}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)},\nabla\Delta\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j-1)})\right|.\end{split}

(6.44)

The case when
D_{t}
in the last term of (
6.41
) acts on
\rho^{({i+1})}
.
Use (
6.42
) with
k=i+1

\begin{split}&\kappa\tau\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int G^{l}_{\tau}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)}\cdot D_{t}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)}\right|=\\
&\kappa\tau\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int G^{l}_{\tau}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)}\cdot\left(\kappa\Delta\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)}+[u\cdot\nabla,\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}]\rho^{(i+1)}+\kappa\nabla\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\left(g_{\tau}\nabla\rho^{(i)}\right)\right)\right|=\\
&\kappa\tau\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int G^{l}_{\tau}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)}\cdot\left(\kappa\Delta\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)}+[u\cdot\nabla,\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}]\rho^{(i+1)}+\kappa g_{\tau}\Delta\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\nabla\rho^{(i)}+\kappa[\nabla\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}},g_{\tau}]\nabla\rho^{(i)}\right)\right|.\end{split}

(6.45)

For the first right-hand side term of (
6.45
) we shift the laplacian:

\begin{split}\kappa^{2}\tau\int G^{l}_{\tau}\Delta\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)}\cdot\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)}=&\kappa^{2}\tau\int G^{l}_{\tau}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)}\cdot\Delta\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)}+[G^{l}_{\tau},\Delta]\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)}\cdot\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)}\\
=&\kappa^{2}\tau\int G^{l}_{\tau}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)}\cdot\nabla\Delta\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)}+\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)}\cdot[\Delta,G^{l}_{\tau}]\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)},\end{split}

so that via the commutator estimate (
6.22
) we have the bound

\begin{split}&\kappa\tau\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int G^{l}_{\tau}\kappa\Delta\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)}\cdot\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)}\right|\lesssim\\
&\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{n+1}\rho^{(i+1)}\|_{L_{xt}^{2}}\kappa\tau\left(\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{m+3}\rho^{(j)}\|_{L_{xt}^{2}}+\|\nabla G^{l}_{\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{m+2}\rho^{(j)}\|_{L_{xt}^{2}}+\|\nabla^{2}G^{l}_{\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)}\|_{L_{xt}^{2}}\right)\\
&\lesssim\VE\rho^{(i+1)}\VE_{n}(\tau\mu)^{j+1}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{m}{2}}D^{\frac{1}{2}},\end{split}

using for the last inequality the inductive assumption and (
6.24
).

The second right-hand side term of (
6.45
) is bounded by the commutator estimate (
6.21
) as follows

\begin{split}\kappa\tau\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int G^{l}_{\tau}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)}\cdot[u\cdot\nabla,\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}]\rho^{(i+1)}\right|&\lesssim\tau\VE\rho^{(j)}\VE_{m}(\|\nabla^{n+1}u\|_{L^{\infty}}\VE\rho^{(i+1)}\VE_{0}+\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\VE\rho^{(i+1)}\VE_{n})\\
&\lesssim(\tau\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}})\VE\rho^{(j)}\VE_{m}\left(\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{n}{2}}\VE\rho^{(i+1)}\VE_{0}+\VE\rho^{(i+1)}\VE_{n}\right)\\
&\lesssim(\tau\mu)^{j+1}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{m}{2}}D^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{n}{2}}\VE\rho^{(i+1)}\VE_{0}+\VE\rho^{(i+1)}\VE_{n}\right),\end{split}

where the second inequality holds by (
6.9
), and the last one by the inductive assumption.

The third right-hand side term of (
6.45
) is, integrating by parts once (
\partial_{\iota}
below is a standard partial derivative,
\iota=1,2,3
, and we sum over repeated
\iota
’s)

\begin{split}&\kappa^{2}\tau\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int G^{l}_{\tau}g_{\tau}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)}\cdot\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\Delta\rho^{(i)}\right|\\
&=\kappa\tau\left|B^{l+1}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\partial_{\iota}\rho^{(j)},\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\partial_{\iota}\rho^{(i)})\right|+\kappa^{2}\tau\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int\partial_{\iota}G^{l+1}_{\tau}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)}\cdot\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\partial_{\iota}\rho^{(i)}\right|\\
&\lesssim\kappa\tau\left|B^{l+1}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\partial_{\iota}\rho^{(j)},\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\partial_{\iota}\rho^{(i)})\right|+(\tau\mu)^{i+j+2}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{n+m}{2}}D,\end{split}

using the inductive assumption and (
6.24
).

The fourth right-hand side term of (
6.45
), via the commutator estimate (
6.22
), is estimated as follows

\begin{split}\kappa^{2}\tau\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int G^{l}_{\tau}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)}[\nabla\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}},g_{\tau}]\nabla\rho^{(i)}\right|&\lesssim\kappa\tau\VE\rho^{(j)}\VE_{m}(\|\nabla^{n+2}g_{\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}\VE\rho^{(i)}\VE_{0}+\|\nabla g_{\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}\VE\rho^{(i)}\VE_{n+1})\\
&\lesssim(\tau\mu)^{i+j+2}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{n+m}{2}}D,\end{split}

using again inductive assumptions and (
6.24
).

Altogether these estimates in (
6.45
) give

\begin{split}&\kappa\tau\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int G^{l}_{\tau}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)}\cdot D_{t}\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)}\right|\lesssim\\
&(\tau\mu)^{j+1}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{m}{2}}D^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\VE\rho^{(i+1)}\VE_{n}+\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{n}{2}}\VE\rho^{(i+1)}\VE_{0}+(\tau\mu)^{i+1}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{n}{2}}D^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\\
&+\kappa\tau\left|B^{l+1}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\partial_{\iota}\rho^{(j)},\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\partial_{\iota}\rho^{(i)})\right|.\end{split}

(6.46)

Estimate of (
6.41
).
Together, (
6.44
), (
6.46
) used for the right-hand side of (
6.41
) give

\begin{split}&\left|B^{l}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)},\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)})\right|\lesssim_{i,m,n,l}\\
&\kappa\tau\left|B^{l+1}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)},\nabla\Delta\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j-1)})\right|+\kappa\tau\left|B^{l+1}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\partial_{\iota}\rho^{(i)},\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\partial_{\iota}\rho^{(j)})\right|+Q(j,m),\end{split}

(6.47)

where we have denoted the parts containing norms of right-hand side by
Q(j,m)
, i.e.

\begin{split}Q(j,m):=(\tau\mu)^{j+1}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{m}{2}}D^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\VE\rho^{(i+1)}\VE_{n}+\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{n}{2}}\VE\rho^{(i+1)}\VE_{0}+(\tau\mu)^{i+1}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{n}{2}}D^{\frac{1}{2}}\right).\end{split}

Using the inductive assumption (
6.36
) part (b) for the first right-hand side term of (
6.47
) we get

\left|B^{l}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)},\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)})\right|\lesssim\kappa\tau\left|B^{l+1}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)},\nabla\Delta\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j-1)})\right|+Q(j,m),

(6.48)

which holds for any
j\leq i
and any multiindex
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n
,
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|=m
. Thus
denoting by

\mathcal{B}(l,m,j):=\sup_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|=m}\left|B^{l}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)},\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(j)})\right|

(with
(i+1)
and
n
fixed) we rewrite (
6.48
) and iterate it as follows

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}(l,m,j)&\lesssim\kappa\tau\mathcal{B}(l+1,m+2,j-1)+Q(j,m)\\
&\lesssim(\kappa\tau)^{2}\mathcal{B}(l+2,m+4,j-2)+\kappa\tau Q(j-1,m+2)+Q(j,m)\\
&\dots\lesssim(\kappa\tau)^{j}\mathcal{B}(l+j,m+2j,0)+\sum_{i=0}^{j-1}(\kappa\tau)^{i}Q(j-i,m+2i),\end{split}

(6.49)

which holds for any
j\leq i
. Observe that
\sum_{i=0}^{j}(\kappa\tau)^{i}Q(j-i,m+2i)\lesssim Q(j,m)
. This in (
6.49
) yields

\mathcal{B}(l,m,j)\lesssim(\kappa\tau)^{j}\mathcal{B}(l+j,m+2j,0)+Q(j,m).

(6.50)

Importantly, we can perform one last step
6
6

6

This iteration “all the way back” allows us to obtain “optimal improvement” mentioned in the introduction.
: recall that we have
\rho^{(-1)}\equiv 0
and observe that (
6.47
) is valid for the choice
j=0
, where it yields

\begin{split}(\kappa\tau)^{j}\mathcal{B}(l+j,m+2j,0)&\lesssim(\kappa\tau)^{j+1}\sup_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|=m+2j}\left|B^{l+j+1}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\partial_{\iota}\rho^{(i)},\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\partial_{\iota}\rho^{(0)})\right|+(\kappa\tau)^{j}Q(0,m+2j)\\
&\lesssim(\kappa\tau)^{j+1}(\tau\mu)^{i+1}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{n+m+2j+2}{2}}D+Q(j,m)\lesssim Q(j,m),\end{split}

where the second inequality is given by the inductive assumption (
6.36
) (b) and via the already established
(\kappa\tau)^{j}Q(0,m+2j)\lesssim Q(j,m)
, an the last inequality follows from the definition of
Q(j,m)
. This estimate used in (
6.50
) with
j=i
yields via
definition of
B

\sup_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|=m}\left|B^{l}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)},\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho^{(i)})\right|\lesssim_{i,m,n,l}Q(i,m).

(6.51)

Substep 5.2: Closing the induction argument.
To close the argument, we need to return to estimates on
\rho^{(i+1)}
, since it appears in
Q(i,m)
. Note that (
6.34
) for
i+1
gives for
n=0

\begin{split}\VE\rho^{(i+1)}\VE^{2}_{0}\lesssim\left|B^{0}(\nabla\rho^{(i)},\nabla\rho^{(i+1)})\right|&\lesssim(\tau\mu)^{2i+2}D+Q(i,0)\lesssim(\tau\mu)^{2i+2}D+(\tau\mu)^{i+1}\VE\rho^{(i+1)}\VE_{0},\end{split}

(6.52)

with the second inequality following from (
6.51
) with
m=n=0
and the last one from the definition of the quantity
Q
. Splitting by Young’s inequality gives the inductive hypothesis (
6.40
) (a’) for
\VE\rho^{(i+1)}\VE_{0}
.

Similarly for
n\geq 1
via (
6.34
)

\begin{split}\VE\rho^{(i+1)}\VE^{2}_{n}\lesssim&\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{n}\left|B^{0}(\nabla\rho^{(i+1)},\nabla\rho^{(i)})\right|+\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=n}\left|B^{0}(\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i+1)},\nabla\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho^{(i)})\right|+(\tau\mu)^{2i+2}\left(\frac{\mu}{\kappa}\right)^{n}D,\end{split}

(6.53)

where we have used the inductive hypothesis (
6.36
) part (a). Observe that at the right-hand sides of (
6.52
) and (
6.53
) the bilinear form
B^{l}
appears only with
l=0
, hence the constants in the (
6.36
) part (a) are
l
-independent. (Since we lower the index of
\rho
at the cost of raising
l
, compare (
6.48
),(
6.50
), closing the argument requires using
B^{l}
,
l>0
.)

Using now for the right-hand side of (
6.53
) the estimate (
6.51
) with
m=n
, the known estimate for
\VE\rho^{(i+1)}\VE_{0}
, and the definition of
Q(i,m)
yields the entire inductive hypothesis (
6.40
) (a’), i.e. the one for
\rho^{(i+1)}
. Now, we use (
6.40
) (a’) for
\rho^{(i+1)}
in (
6.51
) to close the induction. Lemma
6.2
is proven.
∎

7.
Construction of the vector field - Proof of Proposition
2.1

This section is devoted to the proof of Proposition
2.1
. We point out that this proposition is a mild variation of
[
BLSV19
, Proposition 2.1]
, and we follow the proof very closely. On the other hand, for our purposes in this paper, we need to adjust and refine certain parameters used in the construction
[
BLSV19
]
, and for this reason we will need to repeat all the steps.

First, we choose the parameters
\gamma_{T},\gamma_{R},\gamma_{E},\gamma_{L},\bar{N}
and
\alpha_{0}
. The universal constants
M,\bar{e}
will be defined below in Definitions
7.14
and
7.10
. Actually, for the proof below we will require the following inequalities relating these parameters:

\displaystyle\gamma_{L}

\displaystyle<(b-1)\beta\,,

(7.1)

\displaystyle 4\alpha(1+\gamma_{L})+2\gamma_{L}

\displaystyle<2(b-1)\beta+\gamma_{T}+\gamma_{R}\,,

(7.2)

\displaystyle\alpha\gamma_{L}

\displaystyle<\gamma_{T}\,,

(7.3)

\displaystyle 4\alpha(1+\gamma_{L})

\displaystyle<\gamma_{R}\,,

(7.4)

\displaystyle 2\beta(b-1)+1+\gamma_{R}

\displaystyle<\bar{N}\gamma_{L}\,,

(7.5)

\displaystyle b\alpha+\gamma_{T}+b\gamma_{R}

\displaystyle<(b-1)(1-(2b+1)\beta)\,,

(7.6)

\displaystyle b\gamma_{E}

\displaystyle<(b-1)(1-(2b+1)\beta)\,.

(7.7)

We first claim that (
2.9
) allow us to choose
\gamma_{L},\bar{N}
and
\alpha>0
so that (
7.1
)-(
7.7
) are valid. To see this, we can first choose
\gamma_{L}>0
sufficiently small so that (
7.1
) holds and

2\gamma_{L}<2(b-1)\beta+\gamma_{T}+\gamma_{R}

and
\gamma_{T}
,
\gamma_{R}
such that

\gamma_{T}+b\gamma_{R}<(b-1)(1-(2b+1)\beta).

Then we choose
\bar{N}
sufficiently large (depending on
\gamma_{L}
) so that (
7.5
) is valid. Finally, we observe that then (
7.2
), (
7.3
), (
7.4
) and (
7.6
) hold for any sufficiently small
\alpha>0
.

Let us make the following important remark:

Remark 7.1
.

The basic principle in the subsequent estimates in this section will be, as it has been used in
[
BLSV19
]
, that implicit constants may depend on these parameters but do not depend on
\lambda_{q}
and in particular do not depend on the large constant
a\gg 1
in the definition of
\lambda_{q}
(cf. (
2.3
)). Consequently, for sufficiently large
a\gg 1
the implicit constants can be absorbed, so that ultimately we obtain the estimates (
2.4
)-(
2.6
) for
q+1
.

7.1.
Mollification step

Following
[
BLSV19
, Section 2.4]
we define

u_{\ell}:=u_{q}*\psi_{\ell_{q}},\quad\mathring{R}_{\ell}:=\mathring{R}_{q}*\psi_{\ell_{q}}-(u_{q}\mathring{\otimes}u_{q})*\psi_{\ell_{q}}+u_{\ell_{q}}\mathring{\otimes}u_{\ell_{q}}

so that
(u_{\ell},\mathring{R}_{\ell})
is another solution to (
2.1
). However, at variance with
[
BLSV19
, Section 2.4]
, we fix a mollifying kernel
\psi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
which, in addition to the usual requirement
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\psi\,dx=1
, also satisfies

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\psi(x)x^{\theta}\,dx=0\quad\textrm{ for any multiindex $\theta$ with $1\leq|\theta|\leq\bar{N}$}.

(7.8)

The construction and use of such mollifiers (called “deep smoothing operators of depth
\bar{N}
”) is standard, see e.g.
[
Gro86
, Section 2.3.4]
; the case of infinite depth was introduced by Nash
[
Nas56
]
. We point out that if
\bar{N}\geq 2
, then
\psi
cannot be nonnegative.

The key point for introducing these “deep smoothing operators” is the following lemma, a variant of the usual smoothing estimates.

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
\psi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
be a smoothing operator of depth
\bar{N}\geq 1
and such that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\psi=1
. Let
\psi_{\ell}(x):=\ell^{-n}\psi(\ell^{-1}x)
. Then for any real
r,s\geq 0

\|f*\psi_{\ell}\|_{C^{r+s}}\lesssim\ell^{-s}\|f\|_{C^{r}}

(7.9)

and for any
r\geq 0
,
0\leq s\leq\bar{N}

\|f-f*\psi_{\ell}\|_{C^{r}}\lesssim\ell^{s}\|f\|_{C^{r+s}}.

(7.10)

The implicit constants depend on the choice of
\psi
as well as on
r,s
.

Proof.

Concerning (
7.9
) assume first that
r=k
and
s=l
are integers and let
\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}
be multi-indices with
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=k,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|=l
. Then
\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}+\boldsymbol{\beta}}(f*\psi_{\ell})=\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}f*\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\psi_{\ell}
, hence

|\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}+\boldsymbol{\beta}}(f*\psi_{\ell})|\leq C_{l}\ell^{-l}\|f\|_{k}.

If
s=l+\alpha
,
\alpha\in(0,1)
, we write

\displaystyle\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}f*\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\psi_{\ell}(x+z)-\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}f*\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\psi_{\ell}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}f(x-y)\left(\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\psi_{\ell}(y+z)-\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\psi_{\ell}(y)\right)\,dy

\displaystyle=\ell^{-k}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}f(x-y)\left((\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\psi)(y+\ell^{-1}z)-(\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\psi)(y)\right)\,dy,

from which we obtain

\|\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}+\boldsymbol{\beta}}(f*\psi_{\ell})\|_{\alpha}\leq C_{l,\alpha}\ell^{-l-\alpha}\|f\|_{k}.

Finally, if also
r=k+\beta
for some
\beta\in(0,1)
, we obtain the required estimate from interpolation between
r=k
and
r=k+1
.
This concludes the proof of (
7.9
) for
r,s\geq 0
.

Next, by considering the Taylor expansion of
f
at
x
we can write
f(x-y)=Q_{x}(y)+R_{x}(y)
,
where
Q_{x}(y)
is a sum of monomials in
y
of degree
d
with
1\leq d\leq\bar{N}
and
|R_{x}(y)|\lesssim|y|^{s}\|f\|_{C^{s}}
. Moreover, from (
7.8
) we deduce that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}Q_{x}(y)\psi_{\ell}(y)\,dy=0
. Thus,

|f-f*\psi_{\ell}|=\left|\int\psi_{\ell}(y)(f(x-y)-f(x))dy\right|\\
\lesssim\|f\|_{C^{s}}\int\ell^{-n}\left|\psi(\ell^{-1}y)\right||y|^{s}dy\lesssim\ell^{s}\|f\|_{s}\,.

This proves (
7.10
) for the case
r=0
. To obtain the estimate for integer
r=k
, repeat the same argument for the partial derivatives

\partial^{a}f
with
|a|=k
. For general real
r\geq 0
we again proceed by interpolation.
∎

With the help of Lemma
7.2
we obtain the following bounds.

Proposition 7.3
.

For any
N\geq 0
we have

\displaystyle\left\|u_{\ell}\right\|_{C^{N+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\begin{cases}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}^{N+1}&\textrm{ if }N+1\leq\bar{N}\\
\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}^{\bar{N}}\ell_{q}^{\bar{N}-N-1}&\textrm{ if }N+1\geq\bar{N}\end{cases}\,,

(7.11)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathring{R}_{\ell}\right\|_{C^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}\ell_{q}^{-N}+\delta_{q}\lambda_{q}^{1+\bar{N}}\ell_{q}^{\bar{N}-N}\,.

(7.12)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left|u_{q}\right|^{2}-\left|u_{\ell}\right|^{2}\,dx\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}+\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}^{\bar{N}}\ell_{q}^{\bar{N}}\,.

(7.13)

Moreover, if
z_{q}=\mathcal{B}u_{q}
and
z_{\ell}=\mathcal{B}u_{\ell}=z_{q}*\psi_{\ell_{q}}
are the vector potentials, we have in addition

\displaystyle\left\|z_{\ell}-z_{q}\right\|_{C^{N+\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}^{\bar{N}}\ell_{q}^{\bar{N}+1-N-\alpha}\,,

(7.14)

Proof.

The bounds (
7.11
) and (
7.14
) follow directly from (
7.9
) and (
7.10
) together with the classical Schauder estimates on the Calderón-Zygmund operator
\nabla\mathcal{B}
. For (
7.12
) we use the bound (
2.5
) and interpolation to obtain

\|u_{q}\otimes u_{q}\|_{C^{\bar{N}}}\lesssim\|u_{q}\|_{C^{0}}\|u_{q}\|_{C^{\bar{N}}}\leq\|u_{q}\|_{C^{1}}\|u_{q}\|_{C^{\bar{N}}}\lesssim\delta_{q}\lambda_{q}^{\bar{N}+1}\,.

Then we apply (
7.10
) to the decomposition

\|\mathring{R}_{\ell}\|_{C^{N}}\leq\|\mathring{R}_{q}*\psi_{\ell_{q}}\|_{C^{N}}+\|(u_{q}\mathring{\otimes}u_{q})*\psi_{\ell_{q}}-u_{q}\mathring{\otimes}u_{q}\|_{C^{N}}+2\|(u_{q}-u_{q}*\psi_{\ell_{q}})\otimes u_{q}\|_{C^{N}}\,.

∎

Note that at variance with
[
BLSV19
, Proposition 2.2]
we have not used a commutator estimate here.

From (
7.5
) we obtain
\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}^{\bar{N}}\ell_{q}^{\bar{N}}\leq\delta_{q}\lambda_{q}^{1+\bar{N}}\ell_{q}^{\bar{N}}\leq\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}
. Consequently we have

Corollary 7.4
.

For any
N\geq 0
we have the estimates

\displaystyle\left\|u_{\ell}\right\|_{C^{N+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}\ell_{q}^{-N}\,,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathring{R}_{\ell}\right\|_{C^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}\ell_{q}^{-N}\,,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left|u_{q}\right|^{2}-\left|u_{\ell}\right|^{2}\,dx\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}\,,

\displaystyle\left\|z_{\ell}-z_{q}\right\|_{C^{N+\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}\ell_{q}^{1-\alpha-N}\,.

7.2.
Gluing step

The gluing step, introduced in
[
Ise18
]
, proceeds as follows. For each
i\in\mathbb{N}
we set
t_{i}=i\tau_{q}
and let
u_{i}
be the (classical) solution of the Euler equations

\begin{split}\partial_{t}u_{i}+\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits(u_{i}\otimes u_{i})+\nabla p_{i}&=0\,,\\
\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits u_{i}&=0\,,\\
u_{i}(\cdot,t_{i})&=u_{\ell}(\cdot,t_{i})\,.\end{split}

(7.15)

It is well-known (see for instance
[
BLSV19
, Proposition 3.1]
) that there exists a constant
c(\alpha)>0
such that, for each
i\in\mathbb{N}
the solution
u_{i}
is smooth, uniquely defined, and satisfies for any
N\geq 1
the estimates

\|u_{i}(t)\|_{C^{N+\alpha}}\lesssim\|u_{\ell}(t_{i})\|_{C^{N+\alpha}}\quad\textrm{ for all }t\in(t_{i}-T,t_{i}+T)

for
T\leq c\|u_{\ell}(t_{i})\|_{C^{1,\alpha}}^{-1}
, where the implicit constant depends on
N
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
. In particular, from our choice of
\tau_{q}
in (
2.7
) we obtain for any
N\geq 1
(cf.
[
BLSV19
, Corollary 3.2]
)

\|u_{i}(t)\|_{C^{N+\alpha}}\lesssim\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}\ell_{q}^{1-N-\alpha}\,,

provided

\tau_{q}\|u_{\ell}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}}\leq c.

(7.16)

Taking into account Remark
7.1
and (
7.11
), this is ensured by (
7.3
) and by choosing
a\gg 1
sufficiently large.
Following the derivation of the stability estimates in
[
BLSV19
, Proposition 3.3]
and
[
BLSV19
, Proposition 3.4]
, we deduce

Proposition 7.5
.

For
|t-t_{i}|\leq\tau_{q}
and
N\geq 0
we have

\displaystyle\|u_{i}-u_{\ell}\|_{C^{N+\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau_{q}\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}\ell_{q}^{-N-1-2\alpha}\,,

(7.17)

\displaystyle\|z_{i}-z_{\ell}\|_{C^{N+\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau_{q}\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}\ell_{q}^{-N-2\alpha}\,,

(7.18)

\displaystyle\|(\partial_{t}+u_{\ell}\cdot\nabla)(z_{i}-z_{\ell})\|_{C^{N+\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}\ell_{q}^{-N-2\alpha}.

(7.19)

Proof.

Using the equations satisfied by
u_{q}
and
u_{i}
, we obtain the equation for the pressure difference

\Delta(p_{\ell}-p_{i})=\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\bigl(\nabla u_{\ell}(u_{i}-u_{\ell})\bigr)+\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\bigl(\nabla u_{i}{(u_{i}-u_{\ell})}\bigr)+\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\mathring{R}_{\ell},

and deduce

\left\|p_{\ell}-p_{i}\right\|_{C^{1+\alpha}}\lesssim\left\|u_{\ell}\right\|_{C^{1+\alpha}}\left\|u_{i}-u_{\ell}\right\|_{C^{\alpha}}+\|\mathring{R}_{\ell}\|_{C^{1+\alpha}}\,.

Using the equation for
u_{q}
and
u_{i}
we then obtain

\|(\partial_{t}+u_{\ell}\cdot\nabla)(u_{\ell}-u_{i})\|_{C^{\alpha}}\lesssim\left\|u_{\ell}\right\|_{C^{1+\alpha}}\left\|u_{i}-u_{\ell}\right\|_{C^{\alpha}}+\|\mathring{R}_{\ell}\|_{C^{1+\alpha}}\,.

Applying Corollary
7.4
, (
7.16
) and Grönwall’s inequality (for details, compare
[
BLSV19
, Appendix B]
) we then conclude

\|u_{i}-u_{\ell}\|_{C^{\alpha}}\lesssim\tau_{q}\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}\ell_{q}^{-1-2\alpha}\,,

which is (
7.17
) with
N=0
. The case
N\geq 1
follows analogously, following the computations in the proof of
[
BLSV19
, Proposition 3.3]
. Furthermore, the estimates (
7.18
)-(
7.19
) can be deduced in the same manner, following the computations in the proof of
[
BLSV19
, Proposition 3.4]
.
∎

Next, as in
[
BLSV19
, Section 4]
, we partition time using a partition of unity
\{\chi_{i}\}_{i}
, with
\chi_{i}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
and
0\leq\chi_{i}\leq 1
, such that

•

\sum_{i}\chi_{i}\equiv 1
in
[0,T]
,

•

\mathop{\rm supp}\nolimits\chi_{i}\subset(t_{i}-\tfrac{2}{3}\tau_{q},t_{i}+\tfrac{2}{3}\tau_{q})
, in particular
\mathop{\rm supp}\nolimits\chi_{i}\cap\mathop{\rm supp}\nolimits\chi_{i+2}=\emptyset
,

•

\chi_{i}=1
on
(t_{i}-\tfrac{1}{3}\tau_{q},t_{i}+\tfrac{1}{3}\tau_{q})
and
\chi_{i}+\chi_{i+1}=1
on
(t_{i}+\tfrac{1}{3}\tau_{q},t_{i}+\tfrac{2}{3}\tau_{q})
,

•

\|\partial_{t}^{N}\chi_{i}\|_{C^{0}}\lesssim\tau_{q}^{-N}
,

and define

\bar{u}_{q}=\sum_{i}\chi_{i}u_{i}\,,\quad\bar{p}_{q}^{(1)}=\sum_{i}\chi_{i}p_{i}.

Further, we define

\displaystyle\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\chi_{i}\mathcal{R}(u_{i}-u_{i+1})-\chi_{i}(1-\chi_{i})(u_{i}-u_{i+1})\mathring{\otimes}(u_{i}-u_{i+1}),

\displaystyle\bar{p}_{q}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=-\chi_{i}(1-\chi_{i})\left(|u_{i}-u_{i+1}|^{2}-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|u_{i}-u_{i+1}|^{2}\,dx\right),

for
t\in(t_{i}+\tfrac{1}{3}\tau_{q},t_{i}+\tfrac{2}{3}\tau_{q})
and
\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q}=0
,
\bar{p}_{q}^{(2)}=0
for
t\notin\bigcup_{i}(t_{i}+\tfrac{1}{3}\tau_{q},t_{i}+\tfrac{2}{3}\tau_{q})
, where
\mathcal{R}
is the “inverse divergence” operator for symmetric tracefree 2-tensors, defined as

\begin{split}({\mathcal{R}}f)^{ij}&={\mathcal{R}}^{ijk}f^{k}\\
{\mathcal{R}}^{ijk}&=-\frac{1}{2}\Delta^{-2}\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\partial_{k}-\frac{1}{2}\Delta^{-1}\partial_{k}\delta_{ij}+\Delta^{-1}\partial_{i}\delta_{jk}+\Delta^{-1}\partial_{j}\delta_{ik}.\end{split}

(7.20)

when acting on vectors
f
with zero mean on
\mathbb{T}^{3}
. See
[
DLS13
]
,

[
DS17
, Definition 4.2 and Lemma 4.3]
and
[
BLSV19
, Proposition 4.1]
.

Finally, we set

\bar{p}_{q}=\bar{p}_{q}^{(1)}+\bar{p}_{q}^{(2)}.

As in
[
BLSV19
, Section 4.2]
, one can easily verify that

•

\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q}
is a smooth symmetric and traceless 2-tensor;

•

For all
(x,t)\in\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T]

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}\bar{u}_{q}+\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits(\bar{u}_{q}\otimes\bar{u}_{q})+\nabla\bar{p}_{q}=\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q}\\
\\
\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\bar{u}_{q}=0,\end{array}\right.

•

The support of
\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q}
satisfies

\mathop{\rm supp}\nolimits\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q}\subset\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\bigcup_{i}(t_{i}+\tfrac{1}{3}\tau_{q},t_{i}+\tfrac{2}{3}\tau_{q}).

(7.21)

With our choice of parameters
\tau_{q},\ell_{q}
the estimates in
[
BLSV19
, Section 4.3 and Section 4.4]
are modified as follows.

Proposition 7.6
.

The velocity field
\bar{u}_{q}
and its vector potential
\bar{z}_{q}=\mathcal{B}\bar{u}_{q}
satisfy the following estimates

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{u}_{q}-u_{\ell}\right\|_{C^{N+\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau_{q}\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}\ell_{q}^{-1-N-2\alpha}\,,

(7.22)

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{z}_{q}-z_{\ell}\right\|_{C^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau_{q}\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}\ell_{q}^{-\alpha}\,.

(7.23)

for all
N\geq 0
. The new Reynolds stress
\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q}
satisfies the estimates:

\displaystyle\left\|\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q}\right\|_{N+\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}\ell_{q}^{-N-2\alpha}+\tau_{q}^{2}\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}^{2}\ell_{q}^{-N-2-4\alpha}\,,

(7.24)

\displaystyle\left\|(\partial_{t}+\bar{v}_{q}\cdot\nabla)\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q}\right\|_{N+\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau_{q}^{-1}\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}\ell_{q}^{-N-3\alpha}+\tau_{q}\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}^{2}\ell_{q}^{-N-2-4\alpha}\,.

(7.25)

Furthermore, we have the estimate

\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\bar{u}_{q}|^{2}-|u_{\ell}|^{2}\,dx\right|\lesssim\tau_{q}\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}+\tau_{q}^{2}\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}^{2}\ell_{q}^{-2-4\alpha}\,.

(7.26)

Proof.

Using the identity
\bar{u}_{q}-u_{\ell}=\sum_{i}\chi_{i}(u_{i}-u_{\ell})
, the bounds (
7.22
) and (
7.23
) follow directly from (
7.17
) and (
7.18
) in Proposition
7.5
.

As in the proof of
[
BLSV19
, Proposition 4.4]
we write the new Reynolds stress as

\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q}=\partial_{t}\chi_{i}(\mathcal{R}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits)(z_{i}-z_{i+1})-\chi_{i}(1-\chi_{i})(u_{i}-u_{i+1})\mathring{\otimes}(u_{i}-u_{i+1})

and note that
\mathcal{R}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits
is a zero-order operator of Calderon-Zygmund type, for which Schauder estimates are valid. Therefore we obtain, again applying Proposition
7.5
,

\displaystyle\|\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q}\|_{C^{N+\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau_{q}^{-1}\|z_{i}-z_{i+1}\|_{C^{N+\alpha}}+\|u_{i}-u_{i+1}\|_{C^{N+\alpha}}\|u_{i}-u_{i+1}\|_{C^{\alpha}}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}\ell_{q}^{-N-2\alpha}+\tau_{q}^{2}\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}^{2}\ell_{q}^{-2-N-4\alpha}\,.

Next, differentiating the expression for
\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q}
as in the proof of
[
BLSV19
, Proposition 4.4]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|(\partial_{t}+u_{\ell}\cdot\nabla)\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q}\|_{C^{N+\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau_{q}^{-2}\|z_{i}-z_{i+1}\|_{C^{N+\alpha}}+\tau_{q}^{-1}\|(\partial_{t}+u_{\ell}\cdot\nabla)(z_{i}-z_{i+1})\|_{C^{N+\alpha}}

\displaystyle+\tau_{q}^{-1}\|u_{\ell}\|_{C^{1+\alpha}}\|z_{i}-z_{i+1}\|_{C^{N+\alpha}}+\tau_{q}^{-1}\|u_{\ell}\|_{C^{1+N+\alpha}}\|z_{i}-z_{i+1}\|_{C^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle+\tau_{q}^{-1}\|u_{i}-u_{i+1}\|_{C^{N+\alpha}}\|u_{i}-u_{i+1}\|_{C^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle+\|(\partial_{t}+u_{\ell}\cdot\nabla)u_{i}-u_{i+1}\|_{C^{N+\alpha}}\|u_{i}-u_{i+1}\|_{C^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle+\|(\partial_{t}+u_{\ell}\cdot\nabla)u_{i}-u_{i+1}\|_{C^{\alpha}}\|u_{i}-u_{i+1}\|_{C^{N+\alpha}}\,.

Using again Proposition
7.5
we deduce

\|(\partial_{t}+u_{\ell}\cdot\nabla)\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q}\|_{C^{N+\alpha}}\lesssim\tau_{q}^{-1}\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}\ell_{q}^{-N-3\alpha}+\tau_{q}\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}^{2}\ell_{q}^{-2-N-4\alpha}\,.

Finally, following the proof of
[
BLSV19
, Proposition 4.5]
we have

\left|\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|u_{i}|^{2}-|u_{\ell}|^{2}\,dx\right|\lesssim\|u_{\ell}\|_{C^{1}}\|\mathring{R}_{\ell}\|_{C^{0}}\lesssim\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q},

so that

\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|u_{i}|^{2}-|u_{\ell}|^{2}\,dx\right|\lesssim\tau_{q}\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}.

On the other hand

\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|u_{i}-u_{i+1}|^{2}\,dx\lesssim\|u_{i}-u_{i+1}\|^{2}_{C^{\alpha}}.

Using the identity from the proof of
[
BLSV19
, Proposition 4.5]

|\bar{u}_{q}|^{2}-|u_{\ell}|^{2}=\chi_{i}(|u_{i}|^{2}-|u_{\ell}|^{2})+(1-\chi_{i})(|u_{i+1}|^{2}-|u_{\ell}|^{2})-\chi_{i}(1-\chi_{i})|u_{i}-u_{i+1}|^{2}\,,

and Proposition
7.5
, we deduce (
7.26
).
∎

We conclude this section with the following summary of the mollification/gluing steps:

Corollary 7.7
.

Let
(u_{q},\mathring{R}_{q})
be a smooth solution of (
2.1
) satisfying the inductive assumptions (
2.4
)-(
2.6
). Then there exists another smooth solution
(\bar{u}_{q},\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q})
of (
2.1
) with the support condition (
7.21
) such that the following estimates hold:

\displaystyle\|\bar{u}_{q}\|_{C^{N+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}\ell_{q}^{-N}\,

(7.27)

\displaystyle\|\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q}\|_{C^{N+\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}\ell_{q}^{-N-2\alpha}\,,

(7.28)

\displaystyle\|(\partial_{t}+\bar{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q}\|_{C^{N+\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau_{q}^{-1}\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}\ell_{q}^{-N-2\alpha}\,,

(7.29)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\bar{u}_{q}|^{2}-|u_{q}|^{2}\,dx\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}\,,

(7.30)

and moreover the vector potentials satisfy

\|\bar{z}_{q}-z_{q}\|_{C^{\alpha}}\lesssim\tau_{q}\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}\ell_{q}^{-\alpha}\,.

(7.31)

Remark 7.8
.

It is useful to compare these estimates with the corresponding bounds obtained in the mollification/gluing steps in
[
BLSV19
]
, namely the bounds in

[
BLSV19
, (4.7), (4.10), (4.11), (4.12)]
. For this comparison let us denote the respective parameters in
[
BLSV19
]
(defined in our case by the exponents
\gamma_{R},\gamma_{L},\gamma_{t}
) by
\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}^{old},\,\ell_{q}^{old},\,\tau_{q}^{old}
, so that, comparing with
[
BLSV19
, (2.7), (2.19), (2.26)]
, we have

\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}^{old}=3\alpha,\quad\ell_{q}^{old}=(b-1)\beta+\tfrac{3}{2}\alpha,\quad\tau_{q}^{old}=2\alpha(1+\gamma_{L}^{old}).

It is not difficult to see that (
7.27
), (
7.28
) and (
7.30
) are sharper bounds than the corresponding bounds
[
BLSV19
, (4.7), (4.10), (4.12)]
, provided
\gamma_{L}<\gamma_{L}^{old}
and
\gamma_{R}>3\alpha(1+\gamma_{L}^{old})
, in particular if

\gamma_{L}<(b-1)\beta\,\textrm{ and $\alpha>0$ is sufficiently small,}

in agreement with (
7.1
).
In contrast, estimate (
7.29
) would only be sharper than
[
BLSV19
, (4.11)]
if
\gamma_{R}<\gamma_{R}^{(old)}
, a condition which we will not assume, because we will need a better bound from (
7.31
).

7.3.
Perturbation step

The construction of the new vector field
u_{q+1}=\bar{u}_{q}+w_{q+1}
is performed in
[
BLSV19
, Section 5.2]
and
[
BLSV19
, Section 5.3]
. We start by recalling the main steps.

7.3.1.

First we define space-time cutoff functions
\eta_{i}
, adapted to the temporal support of
\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q}
in (
7.21
), and supported in “squiggling stripes”, as done in
[
BLSV19
, Lemma 5.3]
. We start with the following construction, which is independent of
q
:

Lemma 7.9
.

There exists two constants
c_{0},c_{1}>0
and a family of smooth nonnegative functions
\bar{\eta}_{i}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R})
with the following properties:

(i)

0\leq\bar{\eta}_{i}(x,t)\leq 1
,

(ii)

\mathop{\rm supp}\nolimits\bar{\eta}_{i}\cap\mathop{\rm supp}\nolimits\bar{\eta}_{j}=\emptyset
for
i\neq j
,

(iii)

\mathbb{T}^{3}\times(i+\tfrac{1}{3},i+\tfrac{2}{3})\subset\{(x,t):\bar{\eta}_{i}(x,t)=1\}
,

(iv)

\mathop{\rm supp}\nolimits\bar{\eta}_{i}\subset\mathbb{T}^{3}\times(i-\tfrac{1}{3},i+\tfrac{4}{3})
.

Moreover, the function
\bar{\eta}(x,t):=\sum_{i}\bar{\eta}_{i}(x,t)
is
1
-periodic in
t
and satisfies

(v)

\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\eta}^{2}(x,t)\,dx=c_{0}
for all
t
,

(vi)

\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{0}^{1}\bar{\eta}^{2}(x,s)\,ds=c_{1}
for all
x
.

Proof.

We start by following the proof of
[
BLSV19
, Lemma 5.3]
and choose a suitable
h\in C^{\infty}_{c}(0,1)
such that, setting

h_{i}(x,t):=h\left(t-\tfrac{1}{6}\sin(2\pi x_{1})-i\right)

(7.32)

the family of functions
\{h_{i}\}
satisfies (i)-(iv) above, and there exists a geometric constant
c_{0}>0
such that

\sum_{i}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}h_{i}^{2}(x,t)\,dx\geq c_{0}\qquad\textrm{ for all }t.

(7.33)

Then define

\bar{\eta}_{i}(x,t)=\left(\frac{1}{c_{0}}\sum_{i}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}h_{i}^{2}(y,t)\,dy\right)^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}h_{i}(x,t)\,,

and

\bar{\eta}(x,t)=\sum_{i}\bar{\eta}_{i}(x,t).

Then
\bar{\eta}_{i}
satisfies (i)-(iv), whereas

t\mapsto\eta(x,t)
is 1-periodic and satisfies (v). Finally, from (
7.32
) we see that

\bar{\eta}_{i}(x,t)=\bar{\eta}_{i}(0,t-\tfrac{1}{6}\sin(2\pi x_{1}))
, and consequently

\bar{\eta}(x,t)=\bar{\eta}(0,t-\tfrac{1}{6}\sin(2\pi x_{1})).

But then the
1
-periodicity of
t\mapsto\bar{\eta}(x,t)
implies that
\int_{0}^{1}\bar{\eta}^{2}(x,t)\,dt
is independent of
x
. This shows (vi).
∎

The constants
c_{0},c_{1}>0
in Lemma
7.9
determine our choice of
\bar{e}>0
:

Definition 7.10
.

The constant
\bar{e}
in (
2.6
) is defined to be

\bar{e}=\frac{3c_{0}}{c_{1}}
for the universal constants in conditions (vi) and (vii) of Lemma
7.9
.

Now we are ready to define the family of cutoff functions
\eta_{i}
, in analogy with
[
BLSV19
]
: let

\eta_{i}(x,t)=\bar{\eta}_{i}(x,\tau_{q}^{-1}t),\quad\eta(x,t)=\bar{\eta}(x,\tau_{q}^{-1}t).

(7.34)

It is easy to see that
\eta_{i}
have the properties:

•

0\leq\eta_{i}(x,t)\leq 1
,

•

\mathop{\rm supp}\nolimits\eta_{i}\cap\mathop{\rm supp}\nolimits\eta_{j}=\emptyset
for
i\neq j
,

•

\mathbb{T}^{3}\times I_{i}\subset\{(x,t):\eta_{i}(x,t)=1\}
, where
I_{i}=(t_{i}+\tfrac{1}{3}\tau_{q},t_{i}+\tfrac{2}{3}\tau_{q})
,

•

\mathop{\rm supp}\nolimits\eta_{i}\subset\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\tilde{I}_{i}
, where
\tilde{I}_{i}:=(t_{i}-\tfrac{1}{3}\tau_{q},t_{i+1}+\tfrac{4}{3}\tau_{q})
,

•

for any
m,n\in\mathbb{N}
we have the estimate

\|\partial_{t}^{m}\eta_{i}\|_{C^{n}}\lesssim\tau_{q}^{-m}.

(7.35)

7.3.2.

The second step is to introduce a scalar function of time, which acts as the trace of the Reynolds stress tensor. In our case this will be defined as

\sigma_{q}(t):=\frac{1}{3c_{0}}\left(e(t)-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\bar{u}_{q}|^{2}\,dx-\bar{e}\delta_{q+2}\right).

(7.36)

We remark that the notation for this function in
[
BLSV19
]
is
\rho_{q}(t)
, but in this paper we reserve
\rho
to denote density in subsequent sections. Moreover, in
[
BLSV19
]
the definition involves
\delta_{q+2}/2
rather than
\bar{e}\delta_{q+2}
, this difference is related to our sharper inductive estimate (
2.6
). In particular, this leads to the following bound, which follows from (
2.6
) and (
7.26
):

\left|\sigma_{q}(t)-\frac{\bar{e}}{3c_{0}}\delta_{q+1}\right|\lesssim\delta_{q+1}(\lambda_{q}^{-\gamma_{E}}+\lambda_{q}^{-\gamma_{R}}+\lambda_{q}^{-(b-1)\beta})\,.

(7.37)

Next, we introduce, as in
[
BLSV19
, Section 5.2]
the localized versions of the Reynolds stress as

R_{q,i}=\eta_{i}^{2}(\sigma_{q}\mathrm{Id}-\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q}),\quad\tilde{R}_{q,i}=\frac{\nabla\Phi_{i}R_{q,i}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}}{\sigma_{q,i}},\quad\sigma_{q,i}=\eta_{i}^{2}\sigma_{q},

(7.38)

where
\Phi_{i}
is the backward flow map for the velocity field
\bar{u}_{q}
, defined as the solution of the transport equation

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+\bar{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)\Phi_{i}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\Phi_{i}(x,t_{i})

\displaystyle=x.

By our choice of
\eta_{i}
and
\tau_{q}
(cf. (
7.16
)) the backward flow
\Phi_{i}
is well-defined in the support of
\eta_{i}
and satisfies the estimate

\|\nabla\Phi_{i}-\mathrm{Id}\|_{C^{0}}\lesssim\tau_{q}\|\bar{u}_{q}\|_{C^{1}}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{-\gamma_{T}}.

(7.39)

In particular, we have the following analogue of
[
BLSV19
, Lemma 5.4]
:

Lemma 7.11
.

For
a\gg 1
sufficiently large we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla\Phi_{i}-\mathrm{Id}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq 1/2\quad\textrm{ for }t\in\tilde{I}_{i},

(7.40)

\displaystyle|\sigma_{q}(t)-\tfrac{1}{3}\bar{e}\delta_{q+1}|

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{9}\bar{e}\delta_{q+1}\quad\textrm{ for all }t,

(7.41)

and for any
N\geq 0

\displaystyle\left\|\sigma_{q,i}\right\|_{C^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+1}\,,

(7.42)

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{t}\sigma_{q,i}\right\|_{C^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+1}\tau_{q}^{-1}\,.

(7.43)

Moreover, we also have, for any
t\in\tilde{I}_{i}

\left|\frac{R_{q,i}}{\sigma_{q,i}}-\mathrm{Id}\right|=\left|\sigma_{q}^{-1}\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q}\right|\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma_{R}}}{{2}}}.

(7.44)

In particular, for
a\gg 1
sufficiently large and for all
(x,t)

\tilde{R}_{q,i}(x,t)\in B_{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}(\mathrm{Id})\subset\mathcal{S}^{3\times 3}_{+}\,,

where
B_{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}(\mathrm{Id})
denotes the Euclidean ball of radius
1/2
around the identity
\mathrm{Id}
in the space
\mathcal{S}^{3\times 3}
.

Proof.

The proof follows closely the proof of
[
BLSV19
, Lemma 5.4]
. In particular (
7.40
) follows from (
7.39
) and (
7.41
) follows from (
7.37
). The estimates (
7.42
)-(
7.43
) can be obtained as in
[
BLSV19
, (5.13)-(5.15)]
. Indeed, we start with using equation (
2.1
) to estimate

\left|\frac{d}{dt}\int\left|\bar{u}_{q}(x,t)\right|^{2}\,dx\right|=\left|2\int\nabla\bar{u}_{q}\cdot\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q}\,dx\right|\lesssim\delta_{q+1}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q},

so that

|\tfrac{d}{dt}\sigma_{q}(t)|\lesssim\|\tfrac{d}{dt}e\|_{C^{0}}+\delta_{q+1}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}\lesssim\delta_{q+1}\tau_{q}^{-1},

where we assume
a\gg 1
is sufficiently large to absorb the term
\|\tfrac{d}{dt}e\|_{C^{0}}
. Then we use (
7.35
) to conclude the bounds (
7.42
)-(
7.43
). The estimate (
7.44
) follows directly from (
2.14
) and (
7.4
). Consequently, the bound on the range of
\tilde{R}_{q,i}
follows from (
7.39
) and by choosing
a\gg 1
sufficiently large.
∎

7.3.3.

With Lemma
7.11
and the definitions in (
7.38
) we define the new perturbation
w_{q+1}
, precisely as in
[
BLSV19
, Section 5.3]
, as follows:
7
7

7

here we use the calculus identities
\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits[\nabla\Phi^{T}U(\Phi)]=\nabla\Phi^{-1}(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits U)(\Phi)
and
\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(\varphi F)=\varphi\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits F+\nabla\varphi\times F
.

\begin{split}w_{q+1}&=\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\left[\sum_{i}\sum_{\vec{k}\in\Lambda}\sigma_{q,i}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}a_{\vec{k}}(\tilde{R}_{q,i})\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}U_{\vec{k}}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{i})\right],\\
&=\underbrace{\sum_{i}\sum_{\vec{k}\in\Lambda}\sigma_{q,i}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}a_{\vec{k}}(\tilde{R}_{q,i})\nabla\Phi_{i}^{-1}W_{\vec{k}}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{i})}_{w_{o}}+\\
&+\underbrace{\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}}\sum_{i}\sum_{\vec{k}\in\Lambda}\nabla(\sigma_{q,i}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}a_{\vec{k}}(\tilde{R}_{q,i}))\times\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}U_{\vec{k}}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{i})}_{w_{c}}\end{split}

(7.45)

Note that in the formulas above
\vec{k}\in\Lambda
denotes vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
and the corresponding sum is finite. In contrast, the notation introduced in
[
BLSV19
]
is

w_{o}=\sum_{i}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\setminus\{0\}}(\nabla\Phi_{i})^{-1}b_{i,k}e^{\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phi_{i}},\quad w_{c}=\sum_{i}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\setminus\{0\}}c_{i,k}e^{\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phi_{i}},

(7.46)

where

b_{i,k}=\sigma_{q,i}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}a_{k}(\tilde{R}_{q,i})A_{k},\quad c_{i,k}=\frac{-i}{\lambda_{q+1}}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\left[\sigma_{q,i}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\frac{\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}(k\times a_{k}(\tilde{R}_{q,i}))}{|k|^{2}}\right],

the index
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\setminus\{0\}
denotes the Fourier variable, and
A_{k}\in\mathbb{C}^{3}
are complex vectors arising in the Fourier decomposition of Mikado flows, specifically of the functions
\psi_{\vec{k}}
in (
2.19
). In particular, since
\psi_{\vec{k}}
is smooth, the Fourier coefficients
a_{k}
in the expression for
b_{i,k},c_{i,k}
, together with all derivatives, and bounded and have polynomial decay in
k
of arbitrary order (cf.
[
BLSV19
, (5.5)]
). At variance with
[
BLSV19
]
we will make use of this fact in the form

\|a_{k}(\tilde{R}_{q,i})\|_{0}\lesssim|k|^{-\bar{N}-3}\,.

(7.47)

The representation (
7.46
) is useful for obtaining estimates for
w_{q+1}
and for the new Reynolds stress
\mathring{R}_{q+1}
, whereas the representation (
7.45
) will be useful for computing the bulk diffusion coefficient induced by
w_{q+1}
in Section
5
.

7.3.4.

As far as the estimates on
w_{q+1},\mathring{R}_{q+1}
are concerned, in light of Remark
7.8
all estimates in
[
BLSV19
, Section 5.3-5.5]
which do not use transport derivatives remain valid. These are (cf.
[
BLSV19
, Lemma 5.5 and Proposition 5.7]
):

Lemma 7.12
.

There is a geometric constant
\bar{M}
such that

\|b_{i,k}\|_{0}\leq\bar{M}\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}|k|^{-\bar{N}-3}\,.

(7.48)

Moreover, for
t\in\tilde{I}_{i}
and any
N\geq 0

\displaystyle\left\|(\nabla\Phi_{i})^{-1}\right\|_{C^{N}}+\left\|\nabla\Phi_{i}\right\|_{C^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\ell_{q}^{-N}\,,

(7.49)

\displaystyle\left\|\sigma_{q,i}^{-1}R_{q,i}\right\|_{C^{N}}+\left\|\tilde{R}_{q,i}\right\|_{C^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\ell_{q}^{-N}\,,

(7.50)

\displaystyle\left\|b_{i,k}\right\|_{C^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}|k|^{-\bar{N}-3}\ell_{q}^{-N}\,,

(7.51)

\displaystyle\left\|c_{i,k}\right\|_{C^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}|k|^{-\bar{N}-3}\ell_{q}^{-N-1}\,.

(7.52)

Proof.

The estimate (
7.49
) follows from (
7.40
) and (
2.13
). Let us denote
D_{t}=\partial_{t}+\bar{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla
. Since
D_{t}\Phi_{i}=0
by definition, for
N\geq 1
we have

\displaystyle\left\|D_{t}\nabla\Phi_{i}\right\|_{C^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\bar{u}_{q}^{T}\nabla\Phi_{i}\|_{C^{N}}\lesssim\|\nabla\bar{u}_{q}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla\Phi_{i}\|_{C^{N}}+\|\nabla\bar{u}_{q}\|_{C^{N}}\|\nabla\Phi_{i}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau_{q}^{-1}\|\nabla\Phi_{i}\|_{C^{N}}+\tau_{q}^{-1}\ell_{q}^{-N}\,.

We deduce (
7.49
) from here using Grönwall’s inequality.
Next, using (
7.38
) we write

\sigma_{q,i}^{-1}R_{q,i}=\mathrm{Id}-\sigma_{q}^{-1}\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q},\quad\tilde{R}_{q,i}=\nabla\Phi_{i}(\mathrm{Id}-\sigma_{q}^{-1}\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q})\nabla\Phi_{i}^{T}\,.

(7.53)

Then, applying (
2.14
) and (
7.41
) we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\sigma_{q,i}^{-1}R_{q,i}\right\|_{C^{N}}\lesssim\delta_{q+1}^{-1}\left\|R_{q,i}\right\|_{C^{N+\alpha}}\lesssim\frac{\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}}{\delta_{q+1}}\ell_{q}^{-N-2\alpha}\lesssim\ell_{q}^{-N},

where in the last inequality we used (
7.4
). Similarly we obtain the estimate for
\tilde{R}_{q,i}
, leading to (
7.50
).

The estimates (
7.51
) and (
7.52
) follow directly from (
7.42
) and (
7.47
) as well as the above.
∎

Because we require inductive estimates on
\bar{N}\geq 1
derivatives of
u_{q}
,
[
BLSV19
, Corollary 5.9]
is replaced by

Lemma 7.13
.

Under the assumption (
7.1
) and assuming
a\gg 1
is sufficiently large, we have for any
N=0,1,\dots,\bar{N}

\displaystyle\left\|w_{o}\right\|_{C^{N}}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{M}\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\,,

\displaystyle\left\|w_{c}\right\|_{C^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\ell_{q}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\,,

\displaystyle\left\|w_{q+1}\right\|_{C^{N}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\tilde{M}\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\,,

where the constant
\tilde{M}
depends on
\bar{N}
and
\bar{M}
.

Proof.

We use the representation in (
7.46
). First of all, using the chain rule we obtain

\|e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phi_{i}}\|_{C^{m}}\leq\lambda_{q+1}^{m}|k|^{m}\|\nabla\Phi_{i}\|_{C^{0}}^{m}+\sum_{j<m,\theta}C_{j,m}\lambda_{q+1}^{j}|k|^{j}\|\nabla\Phi_{i}\|_{C^{0}}^{\theta_{1}}\cdot\dots\cdot\|\nabla\Phi_{i}\|_{C^{m-1}}^{\theta_{m}}\,

for some constants
C_{j,m}
(binomial coefficients),
where the sum is over
1\leq j\leq m
and multi-indices
\theta
with
m=\theta_{1}+2\theta_{2}+\dots+m\theta_{m}
and
j=\theta_{1}+\dots+\theta_{m}
. Then, using Lemma
7.12
we deduce

\|e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phi_{i}}\|_{C^{m}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{m}|k|^{m}+\lambda_{q+1}|k|\ell_{q}^{1-m}.

However, from (
7.1
) it follows in particular
\gamma_{L}<(b-1)
, hence
\ell_{q}^{-1}<\lambda_{q+1}
, so that we deduce

\|e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phi_{i}}\|_{C^{m}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{m}|k|^{m}.

By applying the product rule and Lemma
7.12
we then conclude that there exists
\bar{M}
such that

\|w_{o}\|_{C^{m}}\leq\tilde{M}\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{m}\quad\textrm{ for all }m=0,1,\dots,\bar{N}.

The estimate on
w_{c}
follows directly from Lemma
7.12
.
∎

Definition 7.14
.

The constant
M
in (
2.5
) is defined as
M:=4\tilde{M}
, where
\tilde{M}
is the constant in Lemma
7.13
.

Finally, coming to estimates involving time-derivatives, we have the following variant of
[
BLSV19
, Proposition 5.9]
:

Lemma 7.15
.

For any
t\in\tilde{I}_{i}
and
N\geq 0
we have

\displaystyle\|D_{t}\nabla\Phi_{i}\|_{C^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q}^{1/2}\lambda_{q}\ell_{q}^{-N}\,,

\displaystyle\|D_{t}\sigma_{q,i}\|_{C^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+1}\tau_{q}^{-1}\ell_{q}^{-N}\,,

\displaystyle\|D_{t}\tilde{R}_{q,i}\|_{C^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau_{q}^{-1}\ell_{q}^{-N}\,,

\displaystyle\|D_{t}c_{i,k}\|_{C^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\tau_{q}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\ell_{q}^{-N-1}|k|^{-\bar{N}-3}\,,

where
D_{t}=\partial_{t}+\bar{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla
.

Proof.

The proof follows
[
BLSV19
, Proposition 5.9]
using this time Lemma
7.11
and Lemma
7.12
. In particular, using the expressions for the
D_{t}
derivatives, we have

\displaystyle\|D_{t}\nabla\Phi_{i}\|_{C^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\Phi_{i}\nabla\bar{u}_{q}\|_{C^{N}}\lesssim\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}\ell_{q}^{-N}\lesssim\tau_{q}^{-1}\ell_{q}^{-N}\,,

\displaystyle\|D_{t}\sigma_{q,i}\|_{C^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\sigma_{q,i}\|_{C^{N}}+\|\sigma_{q,i}\|_{C^{N+1}}\|\bar{u}_{q}\|_{C^{1}}+\|\sigma_{q,i}\|_{C^{1}}\|\bar{u}_{q}\|_{C^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+1}\tau_{q}^{-1}+\delta_{q+1}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}+\delta_{q+1}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}\ell_{q}^{-N+1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+1}\tau_{q}^{-1}\ell_{q}^{-N}\,.

Further, using (
7.38
) we write
\sigma_{q,i}^{-1}R_{q,i}=\mathrm{Id}-\sigma_{q}^{-1}\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q}
and compute

\displaystyle\|D_{t}(\sigma_{q,i}^{-1}R_{q,i})\|_{C^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\sigma_{q}^{-1}\partial_{t}\sigma_{q}\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q}\|_{C^{N+\alpha}}+\|\sigma_{q}^{-1}D_{t}\mathring{\bar{R}}_{q}\|_{C^{N+\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathring{\delta}_{q+1}}{\delta_{q+1}}\tau_{q}^{-1}\ell_{q}^{-N-2\alpha}\lesssim\tau_{q}^{-1}\ell_{q}^{-N}\,,

where we again used (
7.4
).
Then, using Lemma
7.12
,

\displaystyle\|D_{t}\tilde{R}_{q,i}\|_{N}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|D_{t}\nabla\Phi_{i}\|_{C^{N}}\|\sigma_{q,i}^{-1}R_{q,i}\|_{C^{0}}+\|D_{t}\nabla\Phi_{i}\|_{C^{0}}\|\sigma_{q,i}^{-1}R_{q,i}\|_{C^{N}}+

\displaystyle+\|D_{t}\nabla\Phi_{i}\|_{C^{0}}\|\sigma_{q,i}^{-1}R_{q,i}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla\Phi_{i}\|_{C^{N}}+

\displaystyle+\|D_{t}(\sigma_{q,i}^{-1}R_{q,i})\|_{C^{N}}+\|D_{t}(\sigma_{q,i}^{-1}R_{q,i})\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla\Phi_{i}\|_{C^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\tau_{q}^{-1}\ell_{q}^{-N}\,.

The estimate for
D_{t}c_{i,k}
follows again from (
7.47
), Lemma
7.11
, Lemma
7.12
and the above.
∎

7.3.5.

Having obtained the analogous estimates for the perturbation
w_{q+1}
, the estimates on the new Reynolds stress
\mathring{R}_{q+1}
proceed precisely as in
[
BLSV19
, Section 6.1]
.
We set (cf.
[
BLSV19
, (5.21)]
)

\mathring{R}_{q+1}=\underbrace{\mathcal{R}\left(w_{q+1}\cdot\nabla\bar{u}_{q}\right)}_{\mbox{Nash error}}+\underbrace{\mathcal{R}\left(\partial_{t}w_{q+1}+\bar{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla w_{q+1}\right)}_{\mbox{Transport error}}+\underbrace{\mathcal{R}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\left(-{\bar{R}}_{q}+(w_{q+1}\otimes w_{q+1})\right)}_{\mbox{Oscillation error}},

(7.54)

where

\displaystyle\bar{R}_{q}=\sum_{i}R_{q,i}\,.

With this definition one may verify that

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}u_{q+1}+\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits(u_{q+1}\otimes u_{q+1})+\nabla p_{q+1}=\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits(\mathring{R}_{q+1})\,,\\
\\
\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits v_{q+1}=0\,,\end{array}\right.

where the new pressure is given by

p_{q+1}(x,t)=\bar{p}_{q}(x,t)-\sum_{i}\sigma_{q,i}(x,t)+\sigma_{q}(t).

The analogue of
[
BLSV19
, Proposition 6.1]
for estimating the new Reynolds stress is

Proposition 7.16
.

The Reynolds stress error
\mathring{R}_{q+1}
satisfies the estimate

\left\|\mathring{R}_{q+1}\right\|_{0}\lesssim\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\tau_{q}\lambda_{q+1}^{1-\alpha}}\,.

(7.55)

Proof.

We follow the proof of
[
BLSV19
, Proposition 6.1]
and estimate each term in (
7.54
) separately.

Concerning the
Nash error
, we have, as in
[
BLSV19
]
, for any
N\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{R}\left(w_{q+1}\cdot\nabla\bar{u}_{q}\right)\right\|_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\setminus\{0\}}\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}^{1-\alpha}|k|^{\bar{N}+3}}+\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}^{N-\alpha}\ell_{q}^{N+\alpha}|k|^{\bar{N}+3}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\setminus\{0\}}\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}}{\ell_{q}\lambda_{q+1}^{2-\alpha}|k|^{\bar{N}+3}}+\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}}{\ell_{q}^{N+1-\alpha}\lambda_{q+1}^{N+1-\alpha}|k|^{\bar{N}+3}},

where we have used the representation (
7.46
), Lemma
7.12
and the stationary phase estimate
[
BLSV19
, Proposition C.2]
. We claim that it is possible to choose
N\geq 1
so that

\lambda_{q+1}^{N-1}\ell_{q}^{N+\alpha}>1.

Indeed, using (
7.1
), this follows provided

N(b-1)(1-\beta)>b+\alpha(1+\gamma_{L}).

(7.56)

In turn, with this choice of
N
, using (
7.1
) again to obtain
\lambda_{q+1}\ell_{q}>1
, and using
\bar{N}\geq 2
, we deduce

\left\|\mathcal{R}\left(w_{q+1}\cdot\nabla\bar{u}_{q}\right)\right\|_{\alpha}\lesssim\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}^{1-\alpha}}\,.

(7.57)

Concerning the
transport error
we write, for
t\in\tilde{I}_{i}
,

\begin{split}(\partial_{t}+\bar{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)w_{o}=&\sum_{i,k}(\nabla\bar{u}_{q})^{T}(\nabla\Phi_{i})^{-1}b_{i,k}e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phi_{i}}\\
&\quad+\sum_{i,k}(\nabla\Phi_{i})^{-1}(\partial_{t}+\bar{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)\left(\sigma_{q,i}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}a_{k}(\tilde{R}_{q,i})\right)e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phi_{i}}\,.\end{split}

(7.58)

As in
[
BLSV19
]
we obtain, arguing again as above with a sufficiently large
N
satisfying (
7.56
),

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{R}\left((\nabla\bar{u}_{q})^{T}(\nabla\Phi_{i})^{-1}b_{i,k}e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phi_{i}}\right)\right\|_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}^{1-\alpha}|k|^{\bar{N}+3}},

whereas, using Lemma
7.15
,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{R}\left((\nabla\Phi_{i})^{-1}(\partial_{t}+\bar{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)(\sigma_{q,i}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}a_{k}(\tilde{R}_{q,i}))e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phi_{i}}\right)\right\|_{\alpha}\lesssim\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\tau_{q}\lambda_{q+1}^{1-\alpha}|k|^{\bar{N}+3}}.

Moreover, using (
7.46
), we have

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+\bar{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)w_{c}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,k}\left((\partial_{t}+\bar{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)c_{i,k}\right)e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phi_{i}}

and obtain, again using Lemma
7.15
and arguing as above,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{R}\left(\left((\partial_{t}+\bar{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)c_{i,k}\right)e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phi_{i}}\right)\right\|_{\alpha}\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\tau_{q}\ell_{q}\lambda_{q+1}^{2-\alpha}|k|^{\bar{N}+3}}\lesssim\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\tau_{q}\lambda_{q+1}^{1-\alpha}|k|^{\bar{N}+3}}.

Since
w_{q+1}=w_{o}+w_{c}
, we deduce

\|\mathcal{R}\left(\partial_{t}w_{q+1}+\bar{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla w_{q+1}\right)\|_{\alpha}\lesssim\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\tau_{q}\lambda_{q+1}^{1-\alpha}}\,.

(7.59)

Concerning the
oscillation error
we argue precisely as in
[
BLSV19
]
and obtain

\|\mathcal{R}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits\left(-{\bar{R}}_{q}+w_{q+1}\otimes w_{q+1}\right)\|_{\alpha}\lesssim\frac{\delta_{q+1}}{\ell_{q}\lambda_{q+1}^{1-\alpha}}\,.

(7.60)

From (
7.1
) we deduce
\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\ell_{q}^{-1}<\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}
. We also recall
\gamma_{T}>0
, hence
\tau_{q}<\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}
. Consequently, combining (
7.57
), (
7.59
) and (
7.60
) we finally deduce (
7.55
) as required.
∎

7.3.6.

Finally, the new energy can be estimated, following
[
BLSV19
, Section 6.2]
, as

Proposition 7.17
.

The energy of
u_{q+1}
satisfies the following estimate:

\left|e(t)-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left|u_{q+1}\right|^{2}\,dx-\bar{e}\delta_{q+2}\right|\lesssim\frac{\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\,.

(7.61)

Proof.

We argue as in
[
BLSV19
]
. More precisely, we write

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left|u_{q+1}\right|^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left|\bar{u}_{q}\right|^{2}\,dx+2\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}w_{q+1}\cdot\bar{u}_{q}\,dx+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left|w_{q+1}(x,t)\right|^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left|\bar{u}_{q}\right|^{2}\,dx+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left|w_{o}\right|^{2}\,dx+\mathcal{E}_{1},

where, arguing as in
[
BLSV19
]
using stationary phase and Lemma
7.13
,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{E}_{1}|=\left|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int 2w_{q+1}\cdot\bar{u}_{q}+2w_{o}\cdot w_{c}+|w_{c}|^{2}\,dx\right|\lesssim\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}.

Similarly

\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left|w_{o}\right|^{2}\,dx=\sum_{i}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathrm{tr\,}R_{q,i}\,dx+\mathcal{E}_{2},

where, using Lemma
7.11
, Lemma
7.12
and (
7.1
),

\displaystyle|\mathcal{E}_{2}|\lesssim\frac{\delta_{q+1}}{\ell_{q}\lambda_{q+1}}\lesssim\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}.

On the other hand, recalling the definition of
R_{q,i}
in (
7.38
) and using property (v) of
\bar{\eta}_{i}
in Lemma
7.9
as well as the definition of
\sigma_{q}(t)
in (
7.36
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathrm{tr\,}R_{q,i}(x,t)\,dx

\displaystyle=3\sigma_{q}(t)\sum_{i}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\eta_{i}^{2}\,dx=3c_{0}\sigma_{q}(t)=e(t)-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\bar{u}_{q}|^{2}\,dx-\bar{e}\delta_{q+2}.

The statement of the proposition follows.
∎

We conclude this section with:

Proof of Proposition
2.1
.

We need to verify that
u_{q+1}:=\bar{u}_{q}+w_{q+1}
, with
\bar{u}_{q}
from Corollary
7.7
,
w_{q+1}
defined in (
7.45
), as well as
\mathring{R}_{q+1}
defined in (
7.54
) satisfy the inductive estimates (
2.4
)-(
2.6
) with
q
replaced by
q+1
.

First of all note that (
2.5
) follows from Lemma
7.13
, choice of
M
in Definition
7.14
, and by choosing
a\gg 1
sufficiently large.

Secondly, (
2.4
) follows from (
7.55
) and the inequality

C\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\tau_{q}\lambda_{q+1}^{1-\alpha}}<\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\gamma_{R}},

where
C
is the implicit constant in (
7.55
). In light of the inequality (
7.6
), this is satisfied provided
a\gg 1
is sufficiently large.
Similarly, (
2.6
) follows from (
7.61
) and the inequality

C\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+1}}<\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\gamma_{E}},

where
C
is the implicit constant in (
7.61
). In lilght of the inequality (
7.7
) this is satisfied provided
a\gg 1
is sufficiently large.

Finally, the estimate (
2.10
) follows directly from Lemma
7.13
. This concludes the proof of Proposition
2.1
.
∎
